# warbandeer-discord — Code Context

> **Purpose:** Discord bot (Bun + TypeScript, discord.js v14) — a generic, modular core that installs
> feature plugins from a published manifest. The core provides GitHub release announcements for watched
> repos, self-update, and a `/report` command that files GitHub issues from Discord; the World of
> Warcraft features (`/dmf`, `/reset`, `/status`, `/transmog`, and the Darkmoon Faire / weekly-reset /
> realm-watch announcements) are the `@rackbops/plugin-wow` plugin (#107), and character linking is
> `@rackbops/plugin-warbandeer` (#100).
>
> **Fork.** Extracted with full history from [nazumods/wow](https://github.com/nazumods/wow)'s
> `apps/warbandeer-discord` — designed and built there by
> [Nazuraki](https://github.com/nazumods), who gets full credit for the original bot and
> every gotcha documented below. This repo is now the bot's own root (no monorepo, no addon
> release pipeline); self-update was repointed at this fork accordingly — see the **Fork
> gotchas** entry below for what's still open.

## Language

**Link Code**:
A short-lived (~10 min), single-use code minted by `/link` and entered into the Warbandeer
desktop app to authorize a Device Token. Not itself a credential for pushing character data —
only for redeeming one, once.
_Avoid_: linking code, auth code, pairing code

**Device Token**:
The long-lived bearer credential the desktop app receives by redeeming a Link Code, sent on
every later character push. The bot persists only a hash of it, never the plaintext. Distinct
from a Link Code: long-lived and repeatable vs. short-lived and single-use.
_Avoid_: API key, access token

**Account Label**:
An operator-chosen name identifying one WoW account (not a Discord account, not a Battle.net
account) within a Linked Account — e.g. `"Main"` or `"Alts"`. One Discord User can hold several,
one per WoW account or per desktop install that has linked.
_Avoid_: account (bare — always ambiguous between a Discord account and a WoW account)

**Linked Account**:
The persisted association between a Discord User and one Account Label: its Device Token hash,
the Character Snapshot it last pushed, and when. Created by redeeming a Link Code; removed by
`/unlink`. A Discord User's links are a list of these, not a single record.
_Avoid_: link, character link (the *act* of creating one is "linking"; the noun is a Linked
Account)

**Character Snapshot**:
The full character-data payload one push replaces its Linked Account's prior snapshot with —
never a diff or an append. Shaped after the desktop app's own `CharDb` (warband gold, and
per-character level/spec/professions/gear ilvl/currencies/playtime/reputations).
_Avoid_: character data (doesn't say "whole thing, replaced wholesale, not merged")

**Plugin**:
A self-contained feature unit the bot installs from the Plugin Index by name: a single-file bundle
exporting `createPlugin(host)`, contributing slash commands (built on a host-named builder so they
land under `COMMAND_PREFIX`), optional scheduler ticks, and an `activate()` that runs once, after
`takeOver()`. It never reaches `config`, the `Client` or `data/` directly — everything comes through
the Host API. The first one is the Warbandeer connector, now the published `@rackbops/plugin-warbandeer`
(the baked-in `src/warbandeer/` was removed in #100). See `docs/adr/0004`.
_Avoid_: module, extension, integration, feature flag, connector (the connector is *a* plugin —
"connector" names the desktop-app feature, not the packaging)

**Plugin Index**:
The published manifest (`plugins.json`, `PLUGIN_INDEX_URL`) listing every available plugin: npm
package, current version, `hostApiVersion`, intents, command names, env keys, an optional
admin-bundle `adminUrl` + `adminApiVersion` (a plugin's own admin-panel tab — epic #123), release notes.
Generated by the plugins repo's CI, cached by the bot at `data/plugins/index.json`; the source of
everything the host needs before login.
_Avoid_: manifest (bare — ambiguous with a package manifest), registry, catalog

**Host API**:
The object handed to a plugin's `createPlugin`: `name`, its declared `env` slice, `dataDir`, a
prefixed `log`, `storage`, `announce`. The only door the host *offers* a plugin — a
declared-dependency boundary, not a sandbox (a command handler's `interaction.client` still reaches
the live `Client`); versioned by the integer `HOST_API_VERSION`.
_Avoid_: context, container, services, host (bare)

**Plugin State**:
`data/plugins/state.json`, written by the bot after activation: per plugin, enabled / installed /
available / configured / active, the error if any, and the notification, snooze, skip and schedule
bookkeeping. Read by `ops/bot-ops.sh status` and the admin panel. **#225:** a plugin taken out of
`PLUGINS` keeps its entry, `enabled: false`, instead of being dropped.
_Avoid_: plugin list, cache

**Admin tab**:
A plugin's own settings UI inside the admin panel — a small piece of browser code (`dist/admin.js`,
exporting `mountAdmin(root, api)`) it may ship instead of relying on the panel's generic flat
env-key fields. Delivered same-origin at `/plugin-admin/<name>.js`, pinned to the plugin's
installed version (`docs/adr/0005`, epic #123, #165).
_Avoid_: settings tab (bare — distinguishes it from the panel's other sections), plugin UI

**AdminApi**:
The bridge object a mounted admin tab receives: `getEnv`/`setEnv` (scoped to the plugin's own
declared keys — a `secret` one is write-only, never returned; `setEnv` goes through the same guarded `POST /api/env` a manual config
edit uses), `getState` (this plugin's `data/plugins/state.json` entry), `proxyFetch` (a data asset
from the plugin's own published package). The panel-side analog of the bot's Host API — the plugin
owns presentation, the panel keeps authority.
_Avoid_: admin api (bare — ambiguous with the panel's own `/api/*` routes), bridge

**Admin bundle**:
The built `dist/admin.js` an admin tab compiles to (`bun build --target browser`, no discord.js) —
served same-origin from `GET /plugin-admin/<name>.js`, fetched by the panel server from the
plugin's own published npm package on the allowlisted CDN host, never loaded cross-origin by the
browser.
_Avoid_: bundle (bare — ambiguous with the bot's own plugin bundle, `dist/plugin.js`)

**Routing**:
Where a plugin lives, as data the bot owns (ADR-0006, Epic #236): per plugin, per Discord server,
which channels its commands work in (`"all"`, or a list) and which channel it posts to. Stored in
`data/routing.json`, written only by the bot; a webhook URL, being a secret, lives in
`data/routing.secrets.json`, which is created owner-only, and in no other file the bot writes (a
corrupt copy moved aside by `readJsonOrFresh`, or the temp file of a failed write, is that same file
under another name, and is owner-only too -- for a file the bot wrote: one put there by hand keeps
its own mode until the bot's first write replaces it). Per plugin, never per command. #237 added the
model (the shapes, the pure decisions in `src/routing/resolve.ts`, the store); #239 wired command
registration to it. With no `routing.json`, or one that places no plugin, the bot registers as
it always did — to `DISCORD_SERVER_ID`, or globally when that is unset, after emptying once the scopes an
earlier routed run owned (#420) — and once a plugin is placed
it registers per server. #241 added the way in: a request the panel drops in the mailbox
(`routing-set`, `webhook-add`, `webhook-remove`, `discovery-refresh`) is validated by the bot against
what it can see and applied without a restart, and its outcome is recorded in `routing.json`'s
`results`; a file seeded by hand still works. #243 made the rest act: a plugin posts to the channels its
routing names (through a channel's webhook when one is registered, as the bot otherwise), and its
commands run only in the channels its routing lists; a plugin whose routing names no channel posts to
`ANNOUNCE_CHANNEL_ID`, as it always has.
_Avoid_: channel config, command permissions (Discord's own per-channel command permissions are a
different thing, which the bot cannot edit — ADR-0006 decision 7)

**Home server**:
The server named by `DISCORD_SERVER_ID`: where a plugin with no entry in `routing.json` lives, posting
to `ANNOUNCE_CHANNEL_ID`. With `DISCORD_SERVER_ID` unset there is no home server and an unplaced
plugin lives in every server — what global registration does today.
_Avoid_: main server, primary guild

**Placed / unplaced plugin**:
A plugin is **placed** when `routing.json` has an entry for it, and lives in exactly the servers that
entry lists; **unplaced** when it has none, and lives in the home server. A placed plugin whose entry
has no servers lives nowhere (see the gotcha "A plugin with an entry in `routing.json` and no
servers lives nowhere").
_Avoid_: enabled / disabled (that is `PLUGINS=` — whether the bot loads a plugin at all)

**Discovery**:
What the bot can see of Discord, published by the bot as `data/discovery.json` so that the panel,
which is not given the Discord token in its environment (`docker-compose.yml`'s `admin` service has no
`env_file:`), can offer servers and channels by name: the servers, their text
channels and whether the bot can post in each, the outcome of the last command registration in each
server it registered commands in, an invite URL, and per plugin whether it posts and which commands it registered.
`DiscoveryFile` in `src/routing/model.ts` is its shape and `validatePluginRouting` checks a request
against one -- the bot's own check of a panel request (#241), which reads the file back with
`readDiscovery` and refreshes it once first if the request names something it does not show. The bot writes it (`src/routing/live.ts`, #239) after every command registration —
including the boot one, whether or not any routing exists — and refreshes it every 15 minutes, and at once when the bot joins or leaves a server (#259) (a write is skipped when the bot cannot read its servers, so the panel keeps the last copy).
_Avoid_: server list, guild cache

## File Map

| File | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `src/plugins/hostMessage.ts` | Pure validation + payload building for `HostApi.post`/`dm`/`edit` (#736, decision 6): `LIMITS` (content 2000, card title/description/footer/fields/field name/field value, link count/label, a link's url 512 (#508, Discord's own cap, which discord.js's builder does not check), #323's buttons per row / rows / button label / customId), `wrapBareUrls` (wraps a bare `http(s)://` URL in `<...>` so it does not unfurl, leaving an already-wrapped one alone; **#453:** a masked link's destination is wrapped whole — `[text](url)` becomes `[text](<url>)`, Discord's own way to keep a masked link from unfurling, with nothing trimmed and the text left alone; for a bare URL, trailing sentence punctuation (`.`, `,`, `:`, `;`, `!`, `?`, a quote) stays outside the brackets, a markdown marker (`*`, `_`, `~`, `|`) only when the same marker opened right before the URL (`**url**`, but a YouTube id ending in `_` keeps it), and a trailing `)` or `]` only when it has no partner inside the URL (`…/Foo_(bar)` keeps its pair); a URL inside a code span or block — a backtick run and the same-length run that closes it, Discord's inline-code rule — is left alone; quadratic only on a long run of unpaired backticks, which is why `validateHostMessage` refuses content over `CONTENT_MAX` before wrapping as well as after — a few ms at that size), and `validateHostMessage(message, { partial, pluginName })` — `partial: true` (edit) makes every part optional but at least one of content/card/links/buttons must be present, `partial: false` (post/dm) requires non-empty content. **#508:** a card's title and a field's name and value that are only whitespace count as empty, as does whitespace-only content that would be all a post or DM shows (no card; beside a card, or in an edit, where `" "` blanks the text over the message's card, it is kept) — they show as nothing, and Discord is expected to refuse them (inferred, not run against Discord) — with the reason an empty one gets; a card `color` must be a whole number from 0 to 0xFFFFFF (discord.js's `setColor` would also resolve `"Random"` or a hex string). Builds the payload through discord.js's own `EmbedBuilder`/`ActionRowBuilder`/`ButtonBuilder` so their validators run too; a builder throw becomes a fixed refusal reason rather than a raw discord.js error string. `message.buttons` (interactive components, #323) is rendered as action rows of at most 5 in list order, ahead of the one `links` row, at most 5 rows in all; each customId must start with `<pluginName>:` (the calling plugin, so a press routes back through `routeInteractionByPrefix` to its `interactions`), be at most 100 characters and unique in the message; `style` is `primary`/`secondary` (default)/`success`/`danger`. Without a `pluginName` there is no prefix to check, so `buttons` is refused outright (`buttons: []` included); `host.ts` always passes the plugin's name. When `buttons` or `links` is given, `components` is the full new row set (`buttons: []` sends `[]`, which on `edit` clears the rows); `links: []` alone still sends no `components`, as before #323; neither given leaves `components` out, so an edit of other parts keeps the buttons. The returned payload always carries `allowedMentions: { parse: [] }` |
| `src/plugins/delivery.ts` | Live discord.js deps for `HostApi.post`/`dm`/`edit` (#736), over an injected `Client` so `host.ts`'s own decisions unit-test with a fake: `sendPayloadToChannel` (fetch + `isSendable()` guard + send, the same shape as `announce.ts`'s `sendToChannel`), `sendPayloadDm` (`client.users.fetch` + `user.send`, mapping Discord's 50007 "cannot message this user" to a clean `Error("recipient cannot be messaged")`), and `editOwnMessage` (fetches the channel and the message, and refuses one this bot did not author with `Error("message was not sent by this bot")`). **#508:** every other discord.js REST error (`DiscordAPIError`, `HTTPError`) reaches the plugin through `withoutRequest`: its message, `name`, Discord's `code` (a plugin tells 10013 "Unknown User" apart by it), `status`, `method` and stack, never `requestBody` (the message being sent), `rawError` or `url`, so a plugin that serializes a rejection logs none of the message; an error that carries no request propagates unchanged (bar a `cause` or `errors` chain nested past five levels). **#545:** the host's own log sites use it too — `index.ts`'s interaction catch, `commands.ts`'s plugin-command catch, `host.ts`'s plugin interaction and autocomplete catches and its `host.log.error` wrapper (the logged copy only; the plugin's own error object is untouched), and `updateReport.ts`'s delivery failure — because a failed reply to an interaction carries the interaction token in `url` and the reply in `requestBody`. What counts as carrying the request: `requestBody`, or an `http(s)://` URL in `url` (a `RateLimitError`'s) or `path` (the bare `TypeError` Bun throws when the call never reached Discord, whose `path` is the full request URL; an `fs` error's non-URL `path` is left alone); the error is also followed through `cause` and an `AggregateError`'s `errors`, five levels down, a wrapper holding one coming back as a plain copy (message, name, stack, `code`) with the cleaned error in its place and whatever hangs below the fifth level cut off. Any copy is a plain `Error`, not an `instanceof` the original class, and keeps only the `status`, `method` and `code` it has. `withoutRequest` never throws (it runs inside `catch` blocks): an error it cannot read (a throwing accessor or Proxy trap), or one that takes more than 1000 visits to walk (or whose `errors` array is longer than the visits left), is replaced by a fixed placeholder `Error`. Wired into `HostApi` from `src/index.ts`'s `makeHost` |
| `src/index.ts` | Before the `Client` exists at all: `loadPluginIndex()` fetches the Plugin Index, `selectPlugins()` picks the plugins named in `PLUGINS=` — handed the previous boot's `state.json` version pins (`pinsFromState(await readPluginState(...))`, a second read of the file `activate()` reads again for `installPlugins`; a read error that is neither "absent" nor "corrupt" is rethrown by `readJsonOrFresh` (#509) and fails the boot here, with the real error in the log) so an older pinned or last-good version can be kept past an index host-API bump (#222) — and `collectIntents()` unions their declared intents onto `CORE_INTENTS` — only then is `createClient()` called, since discord.js freezes intents at construction. Each skip reason from `describeSkips()` is logged (`[plugins] <name>: <reason>`) before the one-line summary. No plugin code loads at this top-level boot block; an unset `PLUGINS` behaves exactly as before (Guilds intent only). Then login, then `activate()` (after `takeOver()` — the `ClientReady` handler is `createReadyHandler`, `src/ready.ts`, #435: clear the verify timer, `takeOver` only in standby, then `activate`, then — again only in standby — `afterActivate`, which is `finishProbation()` (#548): it waits for the scheduler's first tick, which `startScheduler` returns and `activate()` keeps in `firstTick`, capped at 60 s, and then runs `completeTakeOver()`): install + load the selected plugin bundles (`installPlugins` → `loadPlugins`, the only place a plugin bundle is `import()`ed — after the handoff; each plugin's `HostApi.announce` is `postForPlugin(entry.name, …)` over one `postDeps` built once in `activate()` — #243: the channels its routing names, through a channel's webhook when it has one, `ANNOUNCE_CHANNEL_ID` when routing names none), then the swap record a previous life left (`resolveUpdateAttempt`, #450) BEFORE the interaction handler attaches — so no `/update` can arm a record it would mistake for the dead life's — and so before the scheduler's first tick can read the attempt marker; one bounded daemon call only when a record exists, skipped during a shutdown, and an `interrupted` verdict is saved on the owed report itself, and the report the boot owes is taken there too (`owedReportNow()`), so the report-back at the end of `activate()` delivers only that one and leaves a `/update` armed since to its own restart (`index.test.ts` pins its place before the handler and the scheduler), slash-command registration built from core + plugin commands (`buildCommandBody`) and carried out by `initRouting` + `applyRouting("boot")` (#239, `src/routing/`): with no `routing.json`, or one that places no plugin, the one PUT it always made (guild if `DISCORD_SERVER_ID`, else global; plus, once, the empties an earlier routed run requires, #420) followed by the unchanged `Registered N slash commands` line, inside the same `try`/`catch` that keeps a registration failure from taking the bot down; once a plugin is placed, one PUT per server and one `Registered commands in N servers (...)` line, the interaction routes (chat commands → `handleCommand` with a plugin-command lookup and, since #243, a channel gate — `gateCommand`, which calls `whereOf` only when the channel's own id is not one the plugin's routing lists; core commands never reach it — `/report` modal submits → `handleReportModal`), the `guildCreate` / `guildDelete` listeners (#259: `guildJoined` / `guildLeft`, attached before the registration so a join during it queues behind it on `live.ts`'s chain; the availability pair is deliberately not wired), `activatePlugins()` (which flips each plugin's `running` gate once its `activate()` succeeds, and, if `disposePlugins` began meanwhile, stops activating and releases the plugin in flight, #541) — and `activate()` returns right after it when `isShuttingDown()` (#541: nothing below runs, so no `state.json` write, no HTTP bind and no update report-back; the pending report survives to the next boot, which reports truthfully; `isShuttingDown()`, not `restartPending()`, because a handoff that began meanwhile can still fail and leave this process running, which must then have booted) — then the scheduler (with `pluginTicks(loaded)`) and, right after it, `startRequestDrain` (#241: the request mailbox every five seconds, each drain inside `withCritical`, its beats gated on `isPluginStateReady` and `restartPending`, so none runs before the boot drain below or on the way out) — so the scheduler's first synchronous tick already runs plugin ticks at boot with `running:true`, the gate kept as defence in depth, `writePluginState()` (skipped, with an error log, when `activate()`'s own read of the previous state did not succeed — `previousStateUnread`, #509 — because the catch around that read goes on core-only with an empty default and the write rebuilds the whole file from `previous`: an unreadable file is left as it is, not replaced), and `reportUpdateOutcome()` fired un-awaited (an owed `/update` follow-up must not delay startup), and `reportPluginUpdateOutcome()` likewise (#104 — the owed `/plugins update` follow-up; DM→channel, clearing `pendingReport` via the shared mutator before delivering). **#105:** `consumePluginRequests(livePluginRequestDeps())` is **awaited before `markPluginStateReady()`** (not fire-and-forget) — so a request dropped while the bot was down is honoured before the `pluginRequests`/`pluginUpdates` ticks or a `/plugins` command interleaves (those two ticks wait on the state-ready flag; the other ticks, plugin ticks included, already run from `startScheduler`), and the single-flight consumer never overlaps the first tick's drain. Under `resolveBootMode() === "standby"` (nazumods/wow#879, confirmed against the daemon since #46) it logs in and attaches **none** of that until `takeOver()` has retired the original — both instances hold the same token and Discord delivers events to both sessions, so a standby with handlers would double every reply. Before it logs in, a standby also asks `detectRollback()` (right after `resolveBootMode()`) whether the original is running again — the operator rolled back — and if so steps aside (`stepAside()`) instead of logging in (#548). A standby that never reaches `ClientReady` inside `VERIFY_DEADLINE_MS` writes a `failed` marker and exits 1, so the original gets a reason rather than a timeout. That timer is armed at the module top from the raw env (`bootMode(process.env)`), before the first boot await, so the 90 s counts from there and `loadPluginIndex`'s 5 s and `resolveBootMode`'s 10 s run inside it (#492); a raw standby that `resolveBootMode()` downgrades to `normal` clears it, and `healOwnName()` (#547) runs right after, only for that case: an ordinary boot never touches the socket, and every restart of a swapped-in container pays one bounded inspect because `HANDOFF_FROM` never clears from its env. After `writePluginState` it calls `markPluginStateReady()` (unconditionally, even on a write failure or a skipped write) to release the `pluginUpdates` tick (#103) now that it can't race the boot write. **#446/#545:** the `InteractionCreate` handler writes its one `[interaction]` line through `createInteractionLine` and registers an `onBeforeExit` hook (removed in its `finally`) that writes it with `exiting` if the process exits first; the shutdown handler's `exit` is `(code) => { runExitHooks(); process.exit(code); }`, so the hooks run at the end of the drain, not at the signal; the handler's catch logs `withoutRequest(err)`. The boot plugin-update report-back (`reportPluginUpdateOutcome`) is skipped, with a log line, while `restartRequested()` is true (#419): the boot drain's `update-now` may have a restart deferred by the first tick's critical section, and the report belongs to the boot after it |
| `src/ready.ts` | **#435:** `createReadyHandler(deps)`, the `ClientReady` handler `index.ts` wires — a pure factory with injected `mode`/`takeOver`/`activate`/`afterActivate`/`clearVerifyTimer`/`log`/`describe`, so the one ordering the standby handoff depends on is driven by a test: log the login line, clear the verify timer, `takeOver` **only** in standby mode, then `activate`, then — again only in standby — `afterActivate` (#548: `finishProbation`, the wait for the first tick and the rest of the swap). Both instances hold the same `DISCORD_TOKEN` and Discord delivers every event to both sessions, so activating before the original is retired doubles every reply and announcement. A `takeOver` that rejects propagates and `activate` never runs; an `activate` that rejects propagates and `afterActivate` never runs. |
| `src/ready.test.ts` | **#435:** drives `createReadyHandler` with fakes whose `order` array records the calls — standby is `log, clear, takeOver, activate, afterActivate` in exactly that order, and `afterActivate` runs only after `activate` has settled; normal never calls `takeOver` or `afterActivate` (fakes that throw) and still clears the timer first; a rejecting `takeOver` means `activate` never runs and the rejection reaches the caller; a rejecting `activate` means `afterActivate` never runs; the login line is what `describe(client)` returned; `activate` gets the client the gateway handed over. `index.test.ts` pins that `index.ts` hands the real pieces to it (`afterActivate` being `finishProbation` on the scheduler's first tick, #548) and attaches nothing else to `ClientReady`, and that a standby checks for a rollback right after `resolveBootMode` and before that handler is attached. |
| `src/client.ts` | `CLIENT_OPTIONS` (the mention-safe default, #48) and `createClient(intents = CORE_INTENTS)` — `CORE_INTENTS` (`[Guilds]`) is what an unset `PLUGINS` still gets; `src/index.ts` instead passes `collectIntents(CORE_INTENTS, selectedPlugins)` once plugins are selected. `CLIENT_OPTIONS.intents` itself is never read directly by `createClient` (always overridden by the parameter) — it stays in place only because `CLIENT_OPTIONS` is also the object pinned whole by `client.test.ts`'s `allowedMentions` check |
| `src/config.ts` | Env config from `.env` — pure `resolveConfig(env)` (exported for tests) + the `config` singleton resolved from `process.env`; throws at import time on: a missing or blank required var (`DISCORD_TOKEN`, `ANNOUNCE_CHANNEL_ID`); a `COMMAND_PREFIX` outside `[a-z0-9_-]{1,20}`; a `PLUGINS` token that is not `name` or `name@x.y.z(-pre)`, or one name in two tokens; a `PLUGIN_INDEX_URL` that is not http(s), `file://` or an absolute path; an `HTTP_PORT` outside 1-65535; a `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOST` that is neither a hostname (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`) nor an IP literal in any spelling, without brackets (#319, #494); a `WATCHED_REPOS` entry that is not `owner/repo` (only the lowercase `none` is the off switch, and not mixed into a list; #342, #494). It also resolves `releaseAnnounceChannelId` (falls back to `announceChannelId`), `httpPort` (#220) and `trustedProxyHost` (#319). (The WoW config — region, `DMF_TIMEZONE`, realm, Blizzard creds — moved to `@rackbops/plugin-wow` in #107, which validates them the same way when it loads). Also the `/report` project→repo map (`REPORT_PROJECTS` + `repoForProject`) + `reportRoleId`, the release-watch list (`watchedRepos` from `WATCHED_REPOS`, comma-separated `owner/repo`, defaulting to `[githubRepo]` when blank — distinct from `githubRepo`, which anchors self-update; the exact word `none` makes it `[]`, i.e. release polling off, `none` mixed into a list throws by name (#342), and any other entry must be `owner/repo` — `None`/`NONE` are refused with a hint (#494)), and the self-update config (`gitSha`, `botBranch`, `autoUpdate`, `adminUserIds`); plugin selection — `plugins` (`PluginSelector[]`, from `PLUGINS=` — bare `name` or `name@x.y.z(-pre)` to pin, validated; an exact repeat token is dropped, and one name in two different tokens throws `PLUGINS lists "<name>" more than once`) and `pluginIndexUrl` (from `PLUGIN_INDEX_URL`, defaulting to the Rackbops/rackbops-bot-plugins repo's published manifest — accepts `http(s)://`, `file://`, or an absolute path) |
| `src/logFormat.ts` | **#324.** Opt-in JSON log lines: `LOG_FORMAT=json` makes `src/bootLog.ts` (the first import) call `installJsonConsole`, which swaps `console.log/info/debug` (stdout) and `warn/error` (stderr) for one `{"time","level","msg"}` object per line, `msg` built with `util.format` so an Error keeps its stack inside the line. One swap covers every host line and every plugin line, since a plugin's `host.log` wraps `console` — for the five methods it swaps (`log/info/debug/warn/error`); `console.trace/dir/table/assert/group`, `process.emitWarning` and direct stdout writes stay plain (#495). Unset or `text` changes nothing; any other value logs one text warning and stays text (a log format never stops the bot). **Crash traces stay plain text:** an uncaught exception or unhandled rejection that ends the process is printed by Bun itself, not through `console`, so it is not wrapped; no process-level handler was added, to keep today's exit behaviour exactly. Reserved in `bot-ops.sh`'s `RESERVED_KEYS`, set by hand. Used by the Rackbops Clerk instance (`ops/README.md`, "A Rackbops Clerk instance"). |
| `src/bootLog.ts` | **#60 item 2 / #168, #493:** `index.ts`'s FIRST import, a side-effect module. After the optional `LOG_FORMAT=json` swap it prints `[boot] env file: …` and `[boot] data dir: …` on every boot, **synchronously** (no top-level `await`), so ESM evaluates it to completion before `./config` can throw on a missing `DISCORD_TOKEN` — both lines survive the misconfigured boot they exist to diagnose. It resolves the dir with `resolveDataDir` from `src/dataDir.ts` inside a `try`: a bad `BOT_DATA_DIR` prints one `[boot] couldn't resolve the data dir` line (after the env-file line) and exits 1. Exports the pure `describeEnvFile`/`describeDataDir`. |
| `src/bootLog.test.ts` | The two pure formatters, plus (**#458, #493**) the module body run for real in a child process (`bun src/bootLog.ts`; `bun src/index.ts` with `DISCORD_TOKEN` blank): both lines in order and exit 0; one JSON record each under `LOG_FORMAT=json`; a relative `BOT_DATA_DIR` prints the env-file line, says why, and exits 1; both lines appear before `./config`'s throw; and a source check that the file contains no `await` (comments stripped). |
| `src/interactionLog.ts` | **#328.** One log line per interaction, written from `src/index.ts`'s `InteractionCreate` handler through `createInteractionLine` (#446), a once-only writer, so at most one line lands: in the handler's `finally` once the interaction is handled, or from an exit hook (`restart.ts`'s `onBeforeExit`) when the process exits before the handler returns (`/update` on the exit-75 path, `/plugins … update … now`, a successful self-update's retire), with the outcome `exiting` (a crash or a SIGKILL runs no hook and leaves no line): `describeInteraction` reads only the kind (`command`, `component`, `modal`; autocomplete and anything else is `null`, no line, since it fires per keystroke), the command name plus its subcommand group and subcommand names, or a component's/modal's customId prefix (a plain name as is, anything else JSON-quoted and bounded through `shown`), the user id, and the guild/channel ids (`dm` without a guild); `interactionLogLine` renders `[interaction] <kind> <name> user=<id> where=<dm|guild:<id>/channel:<id>> outcome=<answered|gated|unclaimed|error|exiting> <n>ms`. The outcome comes from `handleCommand`'s return value (`answered`, `gated`, `unclaimed` — a plugin command whose plugin is not running included, #407 — or `error` for a plugin handler's throw), `dispatchPluginInteractionOutcome`'s (`answered`, `error`, `unclaimed`), and `error` for anything that throws out to `index.ts`'s own catch (a core command's or the report modal's handler, or a refusal reply Discord rejects), and `exiting` from the exit hook. **#545:** the error behind an `error` outcome is logged through `withoutRequest`, so a REST error from the reply (or a network failure that never reached Discord, or either as a `cause`) carries no URL (which holds the interaction token) and no request body (the reply). `createInteractionLine(facts, write, now?)` reads `now` once when it is made (the start of the handling) and returns `line(outcome)`, which writes at most once and writes nothing for a `null` `facts` (autocomplete). Never an option value, a modal field or a reply: none is read |

`cmd(name)` builds every command under `config.commandPrefix` (via `commandNamer(prefix)`, now in `src/commandNaming.ts` so `src/plugins/host.ts` can name plugin commands the same way WITHOUT importing `commands.ts` — the #104 cycle break that lets `commands.ts` import `host.ts`'s state mutator), `bareName()` strips it back off on dispatch; `handleCommand(interaction, lookup?, gate?)` runs a core command first, and never puts it to the gate; a non-core one goes to `lookup(bare)` — a loaded plugin's command, as a `PluginCommandTarget` (`findPluginCommand` in `host.ts`) — after `gate(bare, interaction)` (#243: a string is the private, ephemeral refusal shown instead, `undefined` lets it run, and a gate that throws lets it run), or, if nothing matches, warns and refuses it privately with `PLUGIN_NOT_RUNNING_REPLY` (`refuseUnclaimed`, #504) instead of Discord's three-second failure; **#407:** only while the owning plugin is `running`, checked before the gate and again after it (a dispose may begin while it is awaited), a refusal being the private `PLUGIN_NOT_RUNNING_REPLY`; a throw from the plugin's handler is isolated like a component's — logged as `[plugins] <name> command /<command> failed` (the error through `withoutRequest`, #545: a failed reply's error loses its URL, which holds the interaction token, and its request body), answered with a private "Something went wrong." unless the plugin already replied or deferred — while a core command's throw still reaches `index.ts`'s own catch; it resolves to `answered`, `gated`, `unclaimed` or `error` for the interaction log line (#328); `isAdmin()` allowlist gate for `/update` and `/plugins`; `refuseUnlessAdmin(interaction)`, the refusal `/update` and `/plugins` share, names the command from `interaction.commandName` (the prefixed name the user typed, e.g. `/pipupdate`), as do the `/plugins` "isn't an installed plugin" and unknown-subcommand replies; `CORE_COMMAND_NAMES` (the table rows' own bare `name`s, `CORE_COMMANDS.map((c) => c.name)`; no prefix, no `bareName()`) is what `selectPlugins` (`src/plugins/registry.ts`) checks a plugin's declared command names against before any plugin loads; `updateReply(decision, latestSha, { runningSha, reason, redeploy, commandName })` (exported for tests) renders the `/update` acknowledgement — a `redeploy` result present at all means the swap **failed**, since a successful one never returns to reply (the replacement retires this process and delivers the ✅ itself) — the running sha is a **parameter, not a `config` read**, because config resolves env at import time and is one singleton for the whole bun test process, so a formatter reading it is only testable by winning a race with whichever test file imports config first; dispatch passes the interaction's user/channel/application/token to `checkForUpdate` as the follow-up requester. **#449/#503/#505:** the `busy` reply promises no report to the refused caller and names the command from the interaction, every `/update` reply (the check's result or its failure text) goes through `clampReply` once with the daemon's error clamped to `DAEMON_ERROR_MAX` first so the verdict survives, and a reply Discord refuses (the token lasts 15 minutes after the command; a build can take that long) is sent to the requester by DM instead, a failing DM rejecting out as the double failure did. **#504:** `/report` in a DM says `REPORT_DM_REFUSAL` (there is no `member` to read a role from). Also the admin-gated `/plugins` command — subcommands `list` (#103) + `update <name> [at]` / `remind <name> [days]` / `skip <name>` / `cancel <name>` (#104), all deferred + ephemeral, gated once before the subcommand switch. `list` renders `renderPluginsList`; the four actions validate against the index + `state.json` then `mutatePluginState` (the shared race-safe mutator) via pure `planPluginAction`. `update` NOW treats the restart as `/update`'s no-socket fallback does (requested inside the critical section, before its reply; the redeploy path requests none): pin write, then `requestRestart`, then the reply, all inside `withCritical` (the exit is deferred to the section's end, so the write and the reply still land first; requesting before the reply means a reply Discord rejects no longer leaves the pin written with no restart, #503 item 10 -- such a reply is logged through `withoutRequest` inside the section and swallowed, since the restart exits the process when the section closes; every other swallowed `/update`/`/plugins` failure is logged the same way, item 11); `update at:<HH:MM UTC | ISO-8601+offset>` stores a `scheduled` entry instead |
| `src/http.ts` | **#220 (ADR-0007).** The host's HTTP listener, and the only thing in the core that touches `Bun.serve`. `startHostHttp({ port, maxBodyBytes, handle, log, proxy })` binds inside the container (no host port is published; the way in is the instance's tunnel). It passes each request to `handle` with the client IP `clientIpFrom` resolves (#319, `src/net/clientIp.ts`): `CF-Connecting-IP` only from a peer address the required `proxy: TrustedProxy` resolves as trusted (the deployment's own `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOST`), else the socket address — so an untrusted caller on the compose network can never spoof its way into another caller's rate limit or lockout. Bun refuses a declared body over `maxBodyBytes`, and the router refuses a chunked one. `stop()` answers any later request that still reaches `fetch` `503` with `Connection: close`; on Bun 1.4.2 `server.stop()` also closes an idle kept-alive connection (cloudflared keeps its origin connections open) before another request can ride it — measured on Windows and in Linux CI — so that check is the backstop for a version or platform that would hand one over, and `http.test.ts` pins what holds either way (the handler never runs again; the same connection ends in a 503 or a closed socket, never a hang; #494). `index.ts` starts it inside `activate()` after `activatePlugins`, only when `HTTP_PORT` is set, with `handle` = `routeHttpRequest` (`host.ts`) and `proxy` built once from `config.trustedProxyHost` (never defaulted — a caller that drops it is a `bun run check` type error). A bind failure is logged and the bot runs on. Shutdown (the `disposePlugins` closure in `index.ts`) stops it before disposing plugins. Tested on a real port-0 listener in `http.test.ts`; the trust logic itself in `src/net/clientIp.test.ts`. |
| `src/net/clientIp.ts` | **#319**, copied from Rackbops/rackbops-bot-plugins#70 (the two repos don't share a package). `createTrustedProxy({ host, lookup?, now?, log? })` resolves `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOST` and caches the address set, re-resolving at most once per 60 s on a cache miss (never a timer) — or 5 s after a FAILED lookup (#493), one lookup in flight at a time — and never throws: a failed lookup keeps the previous set and is logged once per failure streak, with one line on the first resolve and one on recovery. `canonicalAddress` gives both sides of the comparison one spelling per address (#494): `::ffff:x.x.x.x` and its hex form fold to plain IPv4, and any other IPv6 literal goes through the URL parser (lowercase, compressed; a zone id is dropped on both sides, since Bun 1.4.2 on Windows reports a link-local peer without one). `refresh(timeoutMs?)` resolves after at most that long while the lookup keeps running and lands later; `index.ts` calls `refresh(PROXY_BOOT_REFRESH_TIMEOUT_MS)` (2 s), so a sidecar that is not resolvable yet delays boot by at most that, fail closed meanwhile. `host === undefined` makes `isTrusted` always `false` (fail closed). `clientIpFrom(req, peerAddress, proxy)` returns `CF-Connecting-IP`'s first comma-separated value only when `peerAddress` is trusted and that value is a syntactically valid IP (`node:net`'s `isIP`); otherwise `peerAddress`, then `"unknown"`. Bun's dual-stack `Bun.serve` reports a real IPv4 peer as `::ffff:x.x.x.x`, which is why `canonicalAddress` (above) folds it to plain IPv4 on both sides of the comparison. `src/http.ts` is the only caller. |
| `src/routing/post.ts` | Where a plugin's `announce` goes (#243). `postForPlugin(plugin, message, deps)` takes `announceTargets` and, per target and in order, posts through the channel's webhook when `routing.json` has metadata for it that is not `broken` and `routing.secrets.json` has a URL for it, and as the bot (`announceTo`) otherwise. With no routing, the post goes to the default channel as the bot, or through the default channel's webhook when one is registered (the one rule every target follows). A webhook that fails, for any reason, falls back to the bot for that post (a message over Discord's cap fails both ways, so the fallback only costs a round trip: the plugin's bug); only a 401 or 404 (`gone`) also marks it broken (`markWebhookBroken`, through the pure `withWebhookBroken`: `webhooks[channel].broken` in `routing.json`, only on the entry that was posted through — same `id` and `addedAt` — with `updatedAt` and `updatedBy` untouched). It rejects only when every target failed, with the first error; the others are logged — both through `delivery.ts`'s `withoutRequest`, so the bot path's discord.js error carries no `requestBody` (#508). `liveExecuteWebhook(fetch?)` POSTs `{ content, allowed_mentions: { parse: [] } }` with a 10 s timeout and returns fixed reasons only. The secrets file is read only when a target could use it, and a routing or secrets read that fails posts to the default channel or as the bot. Pure over injected deps; tested in `post.test.ts` (with a token-search test that finds the URL in no log line, error or `routing.json`). |
| `src/routing/gate.ts` | The channel gate (#243). `whereOf(interaction, fetchChannel)` is the only function that touches discord.js objects: a thread's `parentId` is its channel's, an uncached channel is fetched once, and a fetch that fails (or a thread with no parent) sets `parentUnknown`. `refusalMessage(command, channels)` words the refusal, e.g. `` `/rsetlist` works in <#1> or <#2> here. `` (five channels, then "and N more"). `gateCommand(plugin, commandName, here, whereOf, readRouting, log, readDiscovery?)` returns the refusal to show or `undefined`, and never throws (on a refusal only, it reads `discovery.json` through `readDiscovery` and names just the listed channels the bot can still see, #483; with none left it lets the command run and logs a `[gate] every channel listed ... is gone` line; no file, no such server, or a throwing read names them all): the channel's own id decides first, and `whereOf` (a thunk, so the fetch is made only if needed) is called only when that id is not listed; a DM, no plugin (a core command), no entry for the server, `"all"`, a routing read that throws and an unknown parent all let the command run, and the last two leave a `[gate]` line. Tested in `gate.test.ts`. |

The tick set comes from `tickChecks(client, extra)` (exported, pure): the core checks in order (`releases`/`autoUpdate`/`pluginRequests`/`pluginUpdates`/`discovery`) then any plugin ticks (the DMF / weekly-reset / realm checks are the wow plugin's now, #107); `startScheduler(client, extraChecks = [])` threads them in from `src/index.ts`'s `pluginTicks(loaded)` and returns the FIRST tick's promise (#548), which `index.ts` keeps in `firstTick` so a standby's probation can wait on it. `announceTo(client, channelId, message)` is split out of `announce()` (the `[announce]` log line unchanged) so a plugin's `HostApi.announce` reuses the exact send path. `TickCheck` now comes from `src/plugins/contract.ts` (imported type-only), not declared here. Also the `pluginUpdates` core check (#103, after `autoUpdate`): a 15-min-gap + startup-catch-up tick that runs `checkPluginUpdates` to DM admins about newer plugin versions **and (#104) execute a due scheduled update — which restarts the bot** (since #442 the due-schedule half, `runDuePluginSchedules`, runs on every 60 s tick between index polls as well) — gated on `markPluginStateReady()` (set by index.ts after the boot `state.json` write) so its keyed-mutator persist can't race that write; `livePluginUpdateDeps(client)` (exported for its wiring test) wires its injected deps, including `pluginsCommand` (`prefixedName(config.commandPrefix, "plugins")`, the name the notice tells admins to run) and `restartPending`/`requestRestart` from `restart.ts` (so `updates.ts` itself stays restart-free). **#105:** a `pluginRequests` core check runs BEFORE `pluginUpdates` (so a just-queued request applies the same tick), draining the mailbox every tick via `consumePluginRequests(livePluginRequestDeps())` (real fs + the shared index/state/mutator, no client — a panel request has no DM; since #241 the same deps carry a `routing` block -- the routing store, `applyRouting` / `refreshDiscovery`, and the webhook lookup -- and the mailbox is also drained every five seconds by `startRequestDrain`, so this tick check is the backstop); gated on `pluginStateReady` like `pluginUpdates`. **#508:** it waits on the drain through `drain.ts`'s `settledWithin` for at most `PLUGIN_REQUESTS_TICK_WAIT_MS` (2 min, below `TICK_WATCHDOG_MS` so the tick, not its watchdog, ends the wait), logging and going on past it, since it queues behind a timer drain that never settles and the whole tick is one critical section (`guardedTick`'s watchdog frees the next tick, not that hold). And the `discovery` core check (#239, after `pluginUpdates`, before the plugin ticks): a 15-min-gap + startup-catch-up tick (`shouldRefreshDiscovery`, the same shape as `shouldPollReleases`) that QUEUES `refreshDiscovery()` from `src/routing/live.ts` to re-snapshot `data/discovery.json` and does not wait for it — the plugin ticks after it must not wait on a registration's PUTs, which is why `index.ts` starts the scheduler before registering — so on the first tick the refresh lands behind the boot registration; a refresh that rejects is caught and logged (`[tick:discovery]`); before `initRouting` it is a no-op. |

Every exported call runs inside `bounded(ms, what, fn)` (#130): one `AbortController` + ref'd `setTimeout` wrapping the **whole operation, request and response-body read together**, disarmed by `clearTimeout` only once it settles (`DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` 60s; `buildImage` overrides to `BUILD_TIMEOUT_MS` 15min, and every call takes an optional `timeoutMs` so each is testable). Bounding the body matters as much as the request — a daemon that answers with headers then wedges mid-body hangs just as hard, and for `/build` the streamed JSONL body IS the build. Deliberately **not** `AbortSignal.timeout()`: Bun backs that with an **unref'd** timer, so whether it fires at all depends on something *else* holding the event loop open. At runtime something usually does (the scheduler's 60s interval; `resolveBootMode`'s own ref'd `withTimeout` covers the pre-login window where no scheduler exists yet) — but that is an unenforced invariant of unrelated code, and it does not hold under test, which is where the gap was found. A ref'd timer needs no such reasoning. Pure `parseBuildOutput()` because `/build` answers **200 even when the build failed**, with the error buried in the JSONL stream — a line that is valid JSON but not an object (`null`) is skipped as noise, where it used to throw a `TypeError` out of `buildImage` (#513); success is only the absence of an error line, so a cleanly truncated build surfaces later, as `createContainer`'s 404 (`could not start the replacement`), not as `build failed` |

The marker is read before the container state on purpose: a replacement that signalled `ready` is already stopping the original, so what it *said* must outrank how it currently looks. `HANDOFF_RESTART_POLICY_ENV` and `HANDOFF_RESTART_RETRIES_ENV` (#490) name the env keys that carry the original's restart policy and, for `on-failure`, its retry cap onto the replacement. `HandoffMarker.replacementId` is the writer's own 12-hex container id (`ownContainerShortId()`, undefined for a pinned hostname); `sha` is logged by the original at `ready`; `at` is informational |

| `src/redeploy.ts` | The swap itself. `resolveBootMode(env)` (#46) is what `index.ts` actually calls to decide its boot mode: `bootMode(env)`'s raw "standby" is confirmed against the daemon — `HANDOFF_FROM` is baked into the replacement's env at creation and never cleared, so without this every later in-place restart of the swapped-in container (`bot-ops.sh restart`, a reboot, a crash loop; not the ops panel's Restart, which has recreated the container since #277) would re-read "standby" forever against a dead original; downgrades to `normal` only once the named original is confirmed gone (a 404, not merely not-running — see the gotcha on why running-state would race a host reboot), the one daemon call it makes is bounded by `RESOLVE_BOOT_MODE_TIMEOUT_MS` (10s, well under `VERIFY_DEADLINE_MS`) since this is the first daemon dependency on the path *before* `client.login()`, and it stays `standby` on any unconfirmed daemon error (including that timeout) rather than risk two live bots. That 10 s bound runs INSIDE the 90 s verify budget since #492 (the timer is armed at `index.ts`'s module top). A `normal` boot of a container that was CREATED as a standby (`HANDOFF_FROM` still in its env: a swapped-in container whose original is gone) is followed by `healOwnName()` (#547), because nothing else renames it when the swap's own rename never landed: it inspects itself and, when its name ends in `-next`, takes the base name when that is free, or held by a STOPPED container of the same compose project and service (removed first, un-forced); a RUNNING holder is left alone and logged as two bots on one token, another service's holder is left alone, and any daemon error or the same 10 s timeout is logged and the boot continues (`canonical` / `renamed` / `occupied` / `unknown`, never throws). The name it takes is `ownName()`'s: the compose project when the container is that or `<project>-next` (how `ops/install.sh` names an instance, `BOT_OPS_CONTAINER=$PROJECT`, a pre-#567 `-next` one included: container `rackbops-discord-bot-next` is its own name and is left alone, and the replacement of instance `prod` or of instance `next` is healed to `rackbops-discord-bot-prod` or `rackbops-discord-bot-next`), otherwise the name without its `-next` suffixes. The limits: that rule and the `HANDOFF_FROM` gate are all that tell a deployment's own `*-next` name from a swap's replacement, so a compose-only `container_name` ending in `-next` that is not its project's and had already self-updated before #547 would be renamed at its next boot, a replacement whose name equals its project label (a hand-written compose with project `foo-next` and `container_name: foo`) is taken for its own name and not healed, and instances `X` and `X-next` on one daemon are not told apart by the sweep or by `bot-ops.sh`'s swap guard (`healOwnName`'s docstring lists all three; `ops/install.sh`'s `check_instance_name` refuses a new `*-next` instance and warns on a re-run or beside a `-next` sibling, #567). The swap's own stopped original is never that holder: it is `HANDOFF_FROM` itself, and while it exists the boot is `standby` and `completeTakeOver` converges it. `redeploy(sha)`: build (`buildRemote()` = `…/<repo>.git#<sha>`, the whole repo at the exact compared sha — never the branch tip, so a push landing in the fetch window cannot ship newer code stamped with the older `GIT_SHA` — tagged **only** `shaTag()` — never `:latest` at build time, see the gotcha) → prune → inspect what holds the replacement's name (#492: after the build and OUTSIDE the quiesce; a RUNNING holder ends the redeploy there with nothing quiesced or created, a stopped one is remembered) → **quiesce** (`beginHandoff` here, not before the build — the build writes no state and is the one long call, #130) → remove that stopped leftover by id, un-forced, as the first call inside it → create + start the replacement from that same sha tag → poll until `decideHandoffOutcome()` settles; **returns only on failure**, since a successful handoff kills this process mid-await — observing `ready` starts a bounded wait for that death, and outliving it demotes the outcome to `stalled` (reclaim + report) rather than quiescing forever. The verdict is not one failed call: a failed inspect leaves the replacement's state unknown for that poll rather than reading as gone, and `redeploy()` re-reads the marker once more before acting on a poll's verdict other than `ready`, so a late `ready` wins, and a late `failed` is reported with its own reason (#433); the verdict's time is what the generation guard judges a restarted replacement against, never epoch 0 (#431); the result's `replacement` (`none`/`removed`/`left`/`remove-failed`) says what became of the `-next` container (#451). One window stays: a `ready` written between that re-read and the removal, which is the guard's inspect plus the removal call — milliseconds against a healthy daemon, up to two sequential 60 s call bounds against a hanging one. Pure `buildCreateSpec()` derives the replacement from the original's own inspect, a deliberate subset (compose labels replicated, bind/volume mounts re-bound and tmpfs carried as reported, `CARRIED_HOST_CONFIG_KEYS` and `StopTimeout` copied, `User` carried over, `GIT_SHA` **dropped** so the new image's baked sha wins, and `WorkingDir`/`Healthcheck`/`StopSignal`/`Entrypoint`/`Cmd` left to the new image for the same reason; of what is dropped only another mount type, an extra network and a published port are warned by name, #491), `shaTag()`/`latestTag()`, `selectImagesToPrune()` (sha-shaped tags of this repo only — `:latest` and hand-named rollback targets are never candidates), `replacementName()` (derived from `ownName()`: `-next` is a reserved suffix, so `baseName()` strips it and the name never drifts to `<name>-next-next`; with the container's compose project known — the label the replacement copies from the original, which `ops/install.sh` sets equal to the container's name — a container named that project, or `<project>-next`, has the project as its own name, so a pre-#567 install.sh instance called `next` or `canary-next` is not mistaken for a replacement: it self-updates into `<name>-next` (instance `prod-next` becomes `rackbops-discord-bot-prod-next-next`). A bot that is not at its own name — itself named `<name>-next` — is refused by `redeploy()` on its name and labels alone, before the build and with no daemon call beyond the self-inspect, saying to wait a few minutes and then `docker restart` it when `HANDOFF_FROM` is set (the swap that started it may still be finishing), and to give it another `container_name` otherwise, #547/#492). Nothing is ever force-removed by name: a failed create's cleanup removes by id, forced, only when it has an id (the start may have taken effect), and by name un-forced otherwise (never started, so the orphan is `created`; a running holder is someone else's (Docker's 409) and is reported in the error text, "held by a container I did not start and could not remove (it may be running; check docker ps)", with the disposition `none`, never `remove-failed`, whose message tells the requester to remove it by hand). `takeOver()` (runs in the replacement, from `index.ts`'s `ClientReady`, before `activate()`): first looks once more at whether the original is running again (`rolledBack`, the same `detectRollback()` the boot check uses; if it is, the operator rolled back and this container steps aside without writing `ready`, #548), then announces `ready`, then restores the original's restart policy onto itself (`restorePolicy`, #160 — not best-effort: a throw writes `failed` and exits; for `on-failure` the original's retry cap rides in `HANDOFF_RESTART_RETRIES` beside the policy name, #490), then `retireOriginal()` — which **stops** the original and does nothing else: since #548 the original is kept, stopped, as the rollback target (an explicit stop is exempt from `restart: unless-stopped`, so it stays down). `retireOriginal()` inspects the original *before* stopping it to tell a genuine first attempt from a call against an original with nothing left to reclaim it — already stopped, or gone entirely (see the gotcha) — a first attempt lets a genuine `stopContainer` failure propagate, everything else degrades one instead. The rest of the swap is `completeTakeOver()` (#548), run by `finishProbation()` from `index.ts` once `activate()` has returned and the scheduler's first tick has settled or `FIRST_TICK_CAP_MS` (60 s) has passed: it inspects the original (a failed inspect skips everything; a 404 means the original is already gone, so there is nothing to rename and the removal it still issues is harmless), steps aside if the original is running, returns early if this process is shutting down (`restart.ts`'s `isShuttingDown()` — a SIGTERM such as the operator's `docker stop <name>-next` starts a drain in which the first tick can settle, which would otherwise end probation just as the rollback begins), applies `latestTag()` onto this build's image via `tagImage()` (#39 — best-effort, and only now, so a build that dies in `activate()` never becomes what a later recreate deploys), removes the stopped original (not forced; a refused removal looks again and steps aside if the original is running), then takes its canonical name (`takeName()`, which `bot-ops.sh` and the panels filter on). `detectRollback()` (the boot and `takeOver` looks) is true when the original is RUNNING while this standby's own restart policy is not `no` (or empty) and `HANDOFF_RESTART_POLICY` is in its env (so a pre-#160 standby is never misread); the two looks inside `completeTakeOver` ask only whether the original is running. `stepAside()` stops THIS container through the daemon and exits 1 after `STEP_ASIDE_GRACE_MS` (30 s) if no signal arrives, or at once if the stop call itself fails (both exits run the #446 exit hooks first). **#491:** `imageRepo()` parses a registry port and a digest for `shaTag`/`latestTag`/`selectImagesToPrune`; the post-`ready` retirement wait inspects the replacement each poll (each inspect bounded by the time left, but never by less than one poll), so one that died after `ready` is `failed` within a poll with its status and exit code, a `failed` marker written as it died still winning; the marker is cleared right before the create, not at the top of `redeploy()`; the compose service alias is attached at create, so `bot` can resolve to the standby for the seconds before its router is up (item 11, declined: moving it needs a disconnect/connect of the live standby); `probeReplacement()` (#450): one bounded look at `<name>-next` for the boot's swap-record resolution; **#558/#507:** `takeOver` re-reads `state.json` after the stop (`reloadState`) and records `retiredBy` (`recordRetirement`); `completeTakeOver` clears it once the original is gone; `markerFor()` ignores a marker another container wrote. |

a rollback seen right before it makes it step aside with no `ready`, no restore and no stop, and the default look and the default step-aside are exercised against a stubbed daemon; against the real HTTP verbs it stops the original and never tags, removes or renames, #548), `retireOriginal` (a first-attempt stop failure propagates, a retry-against-a-corpse stop failure is tolerated, the ordinary 304 retry path is unchanged, a fully-gone original tolerates a genuine stop failure too and still returns cleanly on the expected 404; a stopped original is left alone, no DELETE and no rename), `completeTakeOver` (inspect, then tag, then a non-forced removal, then the rename to the canonical name — ordering, the DELETE's missing `force`, and the tag call's source and target, against a recording `fetch`; `resolveBootMode` (#46) — no `HANDOFF_FROM` or an empty one stays `normal` without ever calling the daemon, a running original keeps `standby`, a confirmed-gone (404) original demotes to `normal`, an original that exists but isn't running yet **stays** `standby` (not `normal` — pins the check to existence, not running-state), and both a thrown daemon error and one that never resolves (timeout) stay `standby`; the handoff verdict (#431, #433, #434, #451) — one failed inspect mid-poll is tolerated, every poll failing ends in a `timeout` that names the last error, a `ready` marker outranks a failed inspect and a `ready` written during the deciding poll beats the deadline, the backstop catch judges the replacement against the moment it fired, a `StartedAt` equal to the verdict time is removed while a later one on a running container (nanosecond fraction included) is left alone, and the result's `replacement` for each way a swap can end; **#547/#492:** `baseName`/`replacementName` (a drifted name folds back to `<name>-next`), a bot named `-next` refused on its name alone with no daemon call beyond the self-inspect (`docker restart` advice with `HANDOFF_FROM`, `container_name` advice without it; labels that are `null` are no project), a running holder refused after the build with nothing quiesced or created, a stopped leftover removed by id un-forced inside the quiesce, a leftover started between the inspect and the sweep reported as a 409 and never force-removed, the by-name cleanup un-forced and the by-id cleanup forced, an `afterEach` in the cleanup harness asserting `redeploy()` never stops or removes its own id, and a bot whose own `-next` name is its compose project's still self-updating into `<name>-next`, and its un-renamed replacement refused with the restart advice, and `healOwnName` (canonical, a container named after its compose project left alone, a replacement healed to the project's name for an ordinary and for a `-next` instance, a free base name, a stopped same-service holder, a running holder, another service's or an unlabelled or null-labelled holder, a drifted `-next-next`, a daemon error at each step, a hung daemon, the 10 s default bound), and again through the real `docker.ts` calls against a recording `fetch` (the un-forced removal by id, the rename's query, the inspect by name); **#450:** `probeReplacement` (alive for a running and a restarting replacement, stopped for exited and created, absent, unknown for a failing own inspect, a failing inspect by name and a daemon that never answers, which is bounded; an instance named `x-next` in project `x-next` probing `x-next-next`, never itself; and the default seams through a recording `fetch`: one inspect of itself by hostname, one of the replacement by name); **#507/#558/#506:** every existing `takeOver` call stubs the state re-read and the retirement record (`NO_STATE`: the defaults read and write the test dir's `state.json`, which `dataIsolation.test.ts` leaves a probe id in), their order after the stop and before the marker clear, a rejection of either only logged, the defaults against a seeded `state.json`, `completeTakeOver` clearing the record once (removed or already-gone original) and keeping it on a failed removal or a step-aside, `markerFor` and the four reads of `redeploy()` ignoring a marker another container wrote (one warn across the polls; All five GitHub calls (the three here and `update.ts`'s two) carry `AbortSignal.timeout(GITHUB_TIMEOUT_MS)` (10s, overridable per call so each is independently testable) since #88 (`fetchTimeouts.test.ts` also scans `src/` for `fetch(`, `fetchFn(`, `fetchImpl(` and `deps.fetch(` calls with no `signal` -- eleven today -- so a new unbounded call written one of those ways fails that test; it is a source heuristic, not a proof, #500) — the budget is **aggregate**, since `checkReleases` walks `config.watchedRepos` serially: `10N + 30` seconds, so past **26** watched repos a tick that spends its whole GitHub budget outlives `TICK_WATCHDOG_MS`; the watchdog then logs and frees the guard, and the next tick skips the releases walk the hung tick stamped on entry and runs (if due) the later checks it never reached, stamping them so the hung tick's tail skips those in turn — each gap-gated check (releases, self-update, plugin updates, discovery) stamps at poll start and gates on a 15-minute gap, so #87's duplicate-announcement race is not re-opened below about 90 repos: two `checkReleases` walks overlap only once one walk itself outlasts `RELEASE_POLL_GAP_MS` (about 90 repos all hanging to the timeout), and each id is persisted right after its announce even then (#438). Not tighter than 10s: a timed-out `fetchShaRelation` degrades to `"unknown"`, which `decideUpdate` resolves to `"restart"` — a real self-redeploy |
