| `ops/bot-ops.sh` + `ops/README.md` | Operator admin surface originally driven by the **Ops** tab in `apps/warbandeer-desktop` / `wow-companion` (neither is part of this repo — see **Fork gotchas**): whitelisted `status`/`logs`/`restart`/`recreate`/`env-get`/`env-set` over docker+`.env`, invoked over SSH (`recreate` since #277: `up -d --force-recreate` on its own, sharing `recreate_bot()` with `env-set`'s own recreate step so the two can never drift — see the Apply-bar gotcha's #277 note above). Takes `BOT_OPS_CONFIG_DIR` + `BOT_OPS_COMPOSE_FILE` + `BOT_OPS_PROJECT` + `BOT_OPS_CONTAINER` as required inputs (see the config-dir gotcha) — no fallback to its own script location or to any built-in name. Core secrets are never read/written (the whitelist excludes them; a plugin's own `secret` keys are the one exception, write-only — see the #240 gotcha); env-set backs up to `<config-dir>/backups/` (**#227:** pid-suffixed, `.env.bak.<stamp>-<pid>`, so a collision needs the same second AND the same pid) then `up -d --force-recreate`. **`env-set`, `restart` and `recreate` each hold one `flock` on `$CONFIG_DIR` for their whole run (#227)** — see the gotcha below. Each also refuses (`guard_no_handoff_in_progress`, run once the config-dir lock is held, #473) while a `<name>-next` self-update replacement is **alive** (running, restarting, paused, removing or created): *in progress* when `<name>` is running, *finishing* when it is not; an exited or dead leftover does not block them (#490), and the way out of a persistent *finishing* is the runbook in `ops/README.md`. `env-get`, and `env-set`'s change detection, read `.env` with compose's `env_file` semantics via `load_env_values` (last occurrence wins, `export` honoured, whitespace/CR and one layer of matching quotes stripped — issue #44); whitelist membership is checked for every submitted key regardless, but a value's FORMAT is only validated against its regex when the key actually changes. `PLUGINS`/`PLUGIN_INDEX_URL` are whitelisted too (`PLUGINS` accepts a subset of what `resolveConfig` boots: `x.y.z(-pre)` pins, no spaces or empty tokens, and no plugin in two different tokens, `plugins_name_repeated`; an exact repeat such as `foo,foo` is accepted, since the bot drops it; #430). Every format, static or plugin, is matched in the C locale (`format_matches`, #430): under a glibc locale such as en_US.UTF-8 `[a-z]` is a collation range that also takes `é`, and the admin image sets no locale, so an SSH run now judges a value the way the panel's run does; an uncompilable format refuses the value. And `env-get`/`env-set` additionally accept every plugin's own env keys — the keys of EVERY plugin in the bot's cached `data/plugins/index.json` (the CachedPluginIndex wrapper, so `.index.plugins`, not a bare `.plugins`), whether or not that plugin is in `PLUGINS` (#256: the index, not `PLUGINS`, decides which keys are plugin keys, so one `env-set` can turn a plugin on AND set its settings, and a key of a plugin that is off is inert until it is on), merged at runtime with the format/`required`/`secret` the Plugin Index carries (#101), so a plugin's key is validated without hand-mirroring it here (`WARBANDEER_INGEST_PORT` moved off the static whitelist to here once #100 removed the baked-in connector); `secret` plugin keys are write-only since #240 (`env-set` may write one, `env-get` never lists it, `env-schema` reports its `pattern`, `required` and `source` plus `secret: true` + `isSet`, never its value); an unreadable index degrades to static-keys-only with a stderr note, never an error (and since #256 the index is read on EVERY `env-get`/`env-schema`/`env-set`, even with no `PLUGINS` — one `docker exec … cat`, and the note when the bot has not cached the index yet); `status` gains a `plugins` array read from `data/plugins/state.json`. Also `plugin-request` (#105): read one request JSON on stdin, `jq`-validate action/plugin/version/at/days, and write it into the bot's `data/plugins/requests/<epoch-ms>-<action>-<rand>.json` via `docker exec -u bun` — the **first docker-exec WRITE + first `-u bun`** in the script (every other exec is a read-only root `cat`; the write must be `-u bun` or the bot, running as `bun`, couldn't delete the file it consumes). The filename is script-controlled; the untrusted JSON is only ever the `cat >` body. **The write is atomic and owner-only** (#240, found by #241's review gate): the body goes to `<file>.tmp` in the same directory and is `mv`ed to `<file>`, because `cat > <file>` creates the file before filling it and the bot's drain (which lists `*.json` and rejects one that will not parse) could read it half-written after the panel was already told `queued` — the temp name must never end in `.json`; a failed write removes the temp file and the script dies (a 502 for the panel) instead of reporting `queued` — with status 1 and a line of its own after docker's stderr, where older versions exited with docker's own status; and `umask 077` (after `mkdir -p`) keeps a request that may carry a webhook URL owner-only, which the bot, running as `bun` like the write, can still read and delete. **#240** (ADR-0006) adds `routing-get` — `{routing, discovery}` read from `data/routing.json` and `data/discovery.json` (a missing, empty or corrupt file is `null`; `url`/`token`/`secret`/`password` members and any webhook-URL-shaped string are scrubbed; the bot's webhook store is never opened or named) — and four more `plugin-request` actions, `routing-set`, `webhook-add`, `webhook-remove` and `discovery-refresh`, each validated per action before the file is written (a `webhook-add` URL travels on stdin only and is never echoed). Still fully usable standalone by SSHing in and invoking it directly (see `ops/README.md`). **#473/#474:** the handoff guard runs after the config-dir lock; `status`'s `dockerError`; no `user:pass@` in `PLUGIN_INDEX_URL`; backups kept until the operator prunes them |
| `ops/bot-ops.test.ts` | bun tests that spawn the real `ops/bot-ops.sh` (bash + jq from the host, a fake `docker` shim first on PATH that logs its argv, serves fixture files for the `exec … cat` reads, and runs the plugin-request write's `sh -c` for real against a scratch dir) against a throwaway config dir: `env-get`'s compose-style read of `.env` (last duplicate wins, `export`, CRLF, quotes, trimming) and `env-set`'s diff-then-validate order (issue #44) — an unchanged out-of-regex value never blocks, a changed invalid one still fails naming the key, differently-spelled-but-equal values are a no-op with no recreate, an `export`ed key is rewritten in place, a real change backs up + rewrites + recreates once; also pins the script's own `BOT_OPS_PROJECT`/`BOT_OPS_CONTAINER` checks (issue #41) — either one unset or outside the safe charset dies naming the problem, PROJECT checked before CONTAINER, in both cases before any docker call. Also (#101) the `PLUGINS`/`PLUGIN_INDEX_URL` whitelist forms (and #430's pairing of the `PLUGINS` row with `resolveConfig` over one shared corpus, `PLUGINS_CORPUS`: a source pin that runs without bash/jq, an env-set twin that judges each refusal's exit code, `.env` and recreate together, and the repeated-name check keyed to `PLUGINS` only; a C-locale test that runs env-set under a collating `COLLATING_LOCALE` where the box has one, and skips loudly where it has none, as in Git Bash; and an uncompilable manifest format failing closed) and the manifest-merged plugin env keys (the fake `docker` shim serves a fixture `index.json`/`state.json` for the `exec … cat` reads): every index plugin's non-secret keys listed after the static ones in manifest order, whether or not the plugin is in `PLUGINS` (the `(#256)` describe), a secret key never listed by `env-get` (and, since #240, settable but write-only — covered by the `(#240)` describe blocks), diff-then-validate honoured for a changed plugin value, an unavailable index degrading to static-keys-only + stderr note, and `status.plugins`. Skips loudly when bash/jq are absent. Discovered by the root `bun test` like `ops/admin/server.test.ts` |
| `ops/install.sh` | Curl-able bootstrap for a fresh instance (`debug`/`prod`) with no checkout on the host — creates the config dir (`.env` from `.env.example`, never overwritten on re-run, `ADMIN_TOKEN` generated fresh into it), the shared `bin/bot-ops.sh` (refreshed unconditionally from `main`; from another branch only when that branch's own copy is byte-identical to `main`'s, else `install.sh` refuses and names `--force-bin` — see the gotcha, #230), the Dockge-managed stack dir's compose file, and that same stack dir's own `.env` (Compose's interpolation source, not the bot's config — see the gotcha) — the latter two always refreshed. Prints the `up -d --build` invocation, plus the optional `--profile admin` one. See `ops/README.md` |
| `ops/admin/` (`server.ts` + `public/index.html` + `Dockerfile`) | Small per-instance admin web panel — an authenticated wrapper around `ops/bot-ops.sh`'s operations (`status`/`logs`/`restart`/`recreate`/`env-get`/`env-set`/`env-schema`, `routing-get` since #240, and `plugin-request` since #105, which the four routing writes use too since #240; `recreate` since #277 — `POST /api/recreate`, what the page's Restart button actually calls; `POST /api/restart` stays routed for SSH/manual use but nothing in the page calls it), plus a few server-native routes that never shell out (`/api/whoami`, `/api/admins`, `/api/branches`, `/plugin-admin/<name>.js`, `/api/plugin-proxy/<name>`, and — #238 — the page's two stylesheets `/rb-theme.css` and `/admin.css` plus Luma favicon `/favicon.svg`, public at this layer like the page itself). **#228:** `/api/admins` writes are serialised through `AdminStore.mutateDynamic`, one process-level queue with a per-call unique temp name — see the Gotchas entry. **#229:** `/plugin-admin/<name>.js` and `/api/plugin-proxy/<name>` carry an asset's bytes unchanged, never decoded. **#226:** an env key read from the Plugin Index is refused (both server- and bridge-side) unless it matches `ENV_KEY_RE`.  `server.ts`'s auth check, route→subcommand mapping, and request handler are pure/DI'd (`HandlerConfig` injects `runBotOps`, matching `updateReport.ts`'s injected-deliverer shape) so `server.test.ts` covers them without a real subprocess; only the `import.meta.main` block at the bottom does real I/O (spawns `bot-ops.sh`, binds the port) — and it **refuses to start** on two misconfigurations rather than degrading: no `ADMIN_TOKEN` (no door-2 auth), and a **relative `BOT_OPS_CONFIG_DIR`** (issue #60 — `resolveAdminsFile` is absolute-only, since the panel places `admins.json` from that value itself and so isn't covered by `bot-ops.sh`'s own guard; an *unset* value still starts, in bootstrap-only mode). `public/index.html` is the whole page — markup plus two inline scripts (the colour-scheme one in `<head>` and the page script), no build step at deploy — styled by the two committed stylesheets it links (`rb-theme.css`, `admin.css`; own rows below) and organised as four tabs, **Overview** (Needs-attention, status, restart, logs — #246, below), **Plugins** (one accordion card per plugin — #244, below), **Servers** (#246, below) and **Settings** (config, admins, identity), with the token gate and the out-of-date banner above them (#238). The open tab is kept in the URL hash (`#overview`, `#plugins`, `#servers`, `#settings`; written with `history.replaceState`, so switching adds no history entry); any other fragment — including a section id such as `#admins-section`, which used to scroll to that section — opens Overview. Its config form renders from whatever `GET /api/env` returns rather than a hardcoded field list, and its changes are applied from the one Apply bar (#257, below), which posts only the changed keys (pure `planEnvSave`, which `server.test.ts` lifts from the page source and pins — issue #44); the raw `PLUGINS` field is not rendered there (a `name@version` pin is set in `.env`). **One card per plugin (#244, replacing #102's list-plus-separate-Plugin-Settings-section shape):** `GET /api/plugins` merges the Plugin Index (fetched via the injected `listPluginIndex`, a `listBranches`-style on-demand `.env` read + fetch/cache) with `status`'s `plugins` and the current `PLUGINS` — pure `parsePluginIndex`/`mergePluginsView`, unit-tested — and degrades to a `200`+`indexError` (installed-only) when the index can't load; `PluginView` also carries `env` (the manifest's declared settings, widened with `description`/`required`) and `commands`, both defensively built the same way `envKeys` already was. `renderPlugins()` (browser-only, like the old `buildPluginRow`) builds one `buildPluginCard` per plugin: an accordion (`<button aria-expanded aria-controls>` inside an `<h3>`, body `role="region"`) whose header badge/summary is `pluginCardState` (`PLUGIN_CARD_STATE`, replacing `PLUGIN_BADGES`' `pluginStateBadge`; `pluginUpdateBadge` is unchanged) — ten states, pending changes (turning the switch, editing a setting) outranking everything else, including an error; an update badge (row 7) only while the plugin is ON (#225: an off plugin never gets nudged to update code that is not running). Each card's **Turn it on** step is the switch (a tick or untick still applies from the Apply bar as the `PLUGINS` line of its one `POST /api/env` — pure `planPluginsSave` keeps `name@version` pins + manifest order, unchanged) plus the `#105` update block, shown only while enabled. Its **Fill in its settings** step draws one field per `p.env` entry: a core key or one another (earlier, in manifest order — `settingOwners`) card already owns is a stub pointing there; a key with no `GET /api/env-schema` row yet (the #256 index-vs-cache lag) can't be edited from here at all; a secret gets a password field (`type=password`, `autocomplete=new-password`) that shows only `•••••••• Saved on the server` + Replace once one is set — its value is never put in the DOM outside that one field, never `localStorage`, never a message, and can be replaced but not blanked. Never a runtime code install, same as before. Full shape, the mock's markup/CSS and every state/rule: `docs/plans/epics/E236/08c-plugin-cards.md`. **Choose where it lives (#245):** a third step, `buildWhereItLivesStep` (`PLUGIN_ROUTING`, lifted + pinned), inserted between Turn it on and Fill in its settings on EVERY card — placement is bot-owned data independent of on/off. Reads `GET /api/routing`'s `{ routing, discovery }` on its OWN load path (`loadRouting`, called from `showApp`, never folded into `reloadConfig`'s `Promise.all` — a routing-only refresh must never make #275's `rereadFromServer` wait on it, and must never touch `pluginsData`/`loadedEnv`). Three modes, decided purely by `routingStepModel` from the raw files plus the browser clock (`isDiscoveryStale`, `DISCOVERY_STALE_MS = 1h`): `missing` (no discovery at all — read-only, saved placement listed by id since nothing has a name yet), `stale` (`generatedAt` unparseable or too old — read-only, listed by name), `ready` (one row per `discovery.guilds[]`, in order, with the saved placement folded in and a webhook state per channel — `none`/`ok`/`broken`); both non-ready modes offer one `Refresh from Discord` button (`POST /api/discovery/refresh`) and never draw a control. In ready mode, ticking a server reveals **Commands work** (`Anywhere in this server` / `Only in chosen channels`, the latter opening a channel checklist) and, only when `discovery.plugins[name]?.posts === true`, **Posts to** (`channelOptionLabel`: plain / ` · webhook` / ` · webhook stopped working` / ` · the bot can't post here`, never forbidding a choice). A server the routing entry names but discovery no longer sees renders as an unavailable, read-only row and is NEVER included in a sent request (`routingSetBody` walks `model.rows` only — sending an unknown server id would make the bot refuse the WHOLE request, #259) — a "Drop now" ghost button force-sends the current placement to shed it. Every control edit updates `routeState` (declared beside `openCards`, survives `renderPlugins` and a background reload the same way) and debounces into ONE `POST /api/routing` per settled change (`scheduleRoutingSend`, `ROUTING_SEND_DEBOUNCE_MS = 1200ms`; a request already in flight holds a newer change and sends it as ONE follow-up once the first settles — never two in flight for one plugin); an unsendable pick (`Only in chosen channels` with nothing ticked) is never sent at all. After a 200, `awaitRequestResult` polls `GET /api/routing` (`ROUTING_POLL_MS = 2s`) until `routing.results` holds the minted id or `ROUTING_ANSWER_TIMEOUT_MS = 30s` passes, then `requestOutcome` decides purely: the bot's own refusal reason verbatim, a per-server Discord-registration failure/not-yet/no-longer-seen note (reading the FRESH `discovery.guilds[].commands`, since a written `routing.json` and a Discord-side registration failure are different things), or — every sent server registered — a `Live` badge (only while the plugin is active) or "Saved. Applies once the plugin is running." (`Check again`/`Reset to what's saved` ghost buttons cover a timeout/refusal). **Routing controls are invisible to the Apply bar on purpose**: every one carries `data-route-*` only, never `data-plugin`/`data-setting-key`/`data-secret-key`, so `collectPending`/`captureCardControls`/`reapplyCardEdits`/`diffEdits`'s four selectors stay four; `onControlEdited` returns early for anything inside `.route` (right after its existing `.plug__admin` return), so a routing tick neither dismisses a finished Apply message nor refreshes the bar. The footnote under the card list changed accordingly: "Switches and settings apply when the bot restarts, through the bar below. Where a plugin lives applies at once." Full shape: `docs/plans/epics/E236/09-where-it-lives.md`. **The Apply bar (#257)** collects every change that needs a restart — the plugin ticks and every edited config field — into one bar (`#apply-bar`, the last child of `<main>`, outside every tab panel, sticky at the bottom, `role="status"`) and sends them as a single `POST /api/env`, `PLUGINS` first: the pure `planApply` (`APPLY_PLAN`) composes `planPluginsSave` and `planEnvSave`, the pure `applyBarView` / `describeApplyFailure` / `failureWroteNothing` (`APPLY_VIEW`) decide its text and buttons and whether a failed apply re-reads the controls (#272), the DOM half (`APPLY`: `collectPending`, `refreshApplyBar`, `applyPending`, `discardPending`, and — #244 — `reloadConfig`/`captureCardControls`/`reapplyCardEdits`/`refreshCardHeaders`) is lifted and run against a stubbed page whose `querySelectorAll` throws on any selector but the collector's four (`#env-fields [data-key]`, `#plugins-list input[type=checkbox][data-plugin]`, and — #244 — `#plugins-list [data-setting-key]` / `#plugins-list [data-secret-key]`, each additionally filtered against a plugin's own bundle DOM with `!el.closest(".plug__admin")`; its one `querySelector` answers only the open-tab lookup that focus recovery uses), and `syncTagValue` (`TAG_SYNC`) is how a chip change reaches it; there is no Save button and no `confirm()` on apply. `server.test.ts` lifts the blocks the page marks `// NAME:begin` / `// NAME:end` (a test pins each is present exactly once): 27 of them today, every name listed once in `server.test.ts`'s lifted-block test, which fails on a rename or an unlisted addition (`PLUGIN_BADGES` is gone, replaced by `PLUGIN_CARD_STATE`). **`showToast(kind, text)` (#300, std-lib #211's `.rb-toast`):** fired at six sites — `sendRouting` (its own initiating-POST failure, `danger`, matching the same failure class every sibling below already toasts), `awaitRequestResult` and `refreshDiscovery` (#245), and `refreshDiscoveryAll`/`addWebhook`/`removeWebhook` (#246) — never the Apply bar (already its own live region) and never the inline update-action messages; `retryRegistration` gets no toast of its own since it reuses `sendRouting` → `awaitRequestResult`, and every outcome either can reach (`sendRouting`'s own `posted-error`, or `awaitRequestResult`'s `applied`/`refused`/`timeout`) now toasts from one of those two. One `.rb-toast-region` mounts under `#app` on first use; each toast carries `role="alert"` (danger/warning) or `role="status"` (success/info) from the pure `toastRole(kind)`, a `.rb-toast__close` button, and `data-rb-enter` cleared one `requestAnimationFrame` after mount so the theme's entrance transition plays (collapses to a no-op transition under `prefers-reduced-motion`, per the theme). `success`/`info` auto-dismiss after `TOAST_MS` (8s); `warning`/`danger` stay until the close button or Escape (dismisses the newest); at most `TOAST_MAX` (4) show at once, the oldest evicted. **#105** adds the update-action buttons to each card with an available update — **Update now** / **Schedule** (a `datetime-local` → offset-bearing ISO via the pure, pinned `scheduleAtToIso`) / **Remind me in 7 days** / **Skip this version** / **Cancel scheduled update** — plus a "What changed" release-notes block and a compat note. Each POSTs the pure `pluginRequestPayload` (never carrying `requestedBy`) to the new server-native `POST /api/plugins/request`, which — Origin-guarded like `/api/admins`, then `parsePluginRequestInput`-validated (the panel-side mirror of the bot's `requests.ts` `validate`, incl. the anchored no-`/` version gate) — sets `requestedBy` from the **verified Access identity, never the body** (`email:<addr>` or `token`) and shells `bot-ops.sh plugin-request` with the JSON on stdin; `GET /api/plugins` now also surfaces each plugin's `scheduled`/`skippedVersion`/`remindAt` (the Cancel/marker inputs) and a `compatible`/`neededHostApi` computed against `HOST_API_VERSION` (a drift-pinned mirror of `contract.ts`). The DOM render (`buildPluginUpdateBlock`) stays browser-only like `buildPluginCard` (#244, which embeds it in each card's **Turn it on** step, shown only while the plugin is enabled); its pure inputs (`pluginUpdateBadge`, `compatible`, `scheduled`) and both boundaries (`parsePluginRequestInput`, the lifted `sendPluginRequest`) are the tested seams. **Admin tabs (#123, #165):** `GET /plugin-admin/<name>.js?v=` serves a plugin's own `dist/admin.js` same-origin (fetched server-side from `ADMIN_ASSET_HOST`, size-capped, public at this layer like the page — before the `/api/` auth gate) and `GET /api/plugin-proxy/<name>?path=&v=` proxies a data asset from that plugin's own package (authed, under `/api/`); both accept `?v=<installedVersion>` to pin delivery to the running code rather than the index's current release. `mountPluginAdmin` (client) imports the bundle and mounts it via `makeAdminApi`'s bridge (`getEnv`/`setEnv`/`getState`/`proxyFetch`), gated by `adminTabState`/`bundleMountDecision` (`PLUGIN_ADMIN_HELPERS`, lifted + pinned) — unchanged by #244 except WHERE it mounts: into a `.plug__admin` root inside the owning plugin's own card, on first open, instead of a separate Plugin Settings section (`renderPluginAdmin`, deleted). **#242 routing routes:** `GET /api/routing` is a `buildInvocation` branch (`routing-get`); the four writes (`POST /api/routing`, `POST /api/webhooks`, `DELETE /api/webhooks/<channel id>`, `POST /api/discovery/refresh`) are server-native `plugin-request` actions — `parseRoutingSetInput` / `parseWebhookAddInput` rebuild the body (since #219 a server's `destinations` too: each name tested against `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$` before it becomes a key, each channel a snowflake, anything else `bad destinations`, left out when empty — the card's step draws one "Where it usually posts" channel picker per destination `discovery.plugins[name].destinations` declares, and `routingSetBody` sends a pick only while its channel is still that row's, the same rule as `postTo`), `queueRoutingRequest` adds `requestedBy` (verified identity) and a server-minted `id` and queues it on stdin, `redactWebhookUrls` cleans anything bot-ops wrote (a webhook URL never leaves the stdin payload). **A Servers tab, a Needs-attention list, and pickers instead of pasted ids (#246):** a fourth tab, **Servers**, one `section.rb-card.server` per `discovery.guilds[]` entry (`serverCardModel`, `SERVERS_TAB`, lifted + pinned) plus one read-only card per server `routing.plugins[*].servers`/`routing.webhooks[*].guildId` still names but discovery does not (`unavailableServers`). Each card shows its commands state (`live`/`refused`/`unregistered`/`single-mode`, folded with #245's routed/single-mode rule — a `null` outside routed mode is normal, never a problem), which plugins live there (placed ones by scope/post-target/webhook state, plus every UNPLACED plugin `byDefault: true` — on the home card when one is configured, or on EVERY card when it is not, per `src/routing/resolve.ts`'s `pluginsForGuild`, a round-1 review-gate finding), its webhooks, and an **Add a webhook** field. **Add / Remove / Try again / Refresh from Discord all share one poller**, `pollForResult(id, startedAt)` (browser-only, beside #245's `awaitRequestResult` — re-reads `GET /api/routing` every `ROUTING_POLL_MS` until `routing.results` holds `id` or `ROUTING_ANSWER_TIMEOUT_MS` passes, clearing on `unauthorized`); busy/settled state per action lives in `serverActionState` (a `Map` keyed `add:<guildId>` / `remove:<channelId>` / `retry` / `discovery` / `copy` — the last the transient "Copied." confirmation, always cleared at the top of the next durable action and in `loadRouting`'s tail and ranked last by the pure `serversStatusMessage`, so it can never mask a settled Refresh outcome; read fresh at every `renderServers()` — the same "never a closure over a DOM node a rebuild replaced" rule as #245's `routeState`). **Try again** (`retryRegistration`) re-sends a placed plugin's OWN saved placement via `ensureRouteState` + `sendRouting` — meant as a no-op that makes the bot re-run `applyRouting` for every server. `retryablePlacement()` picks the first PLACED plugin (in `pluginsData` order) that is both `ready` AND has zero servers the bot has left (`model.unavailable.length === 0`), skipping over any ready-but-blocked one instead of just taking the first placed plugin outright: `routingSetBody` walks `model.rows` only (discovery's current guilds), and a `routing-set` REPLACES the plugin's whole entry on the bot, so resending a plugin still placed in an unavailable server would silently drop that server's placement for good (an orchestrator-round finding, since the button's own label promised a no-op). Four states, not just button-or-sentence: a safe placement draws the button; every ready placed plugin being blocked draws a note naming which and how many servers would be dropped, pointing at "Drop now" on that card first; nothing yet `ready` (still `stale`/`missing`) draws "Routing data is stale, so Try again is unavailable until Discord refreshes"; nothing placed at all draws "Nothing is placed yet… Restart the bot (Overview) to retry." `ensureRouteState`'s seed was extracted into a pure `selectionFromModel(model)` inside `PLUGIN_ROUTING` (behaviour unchanged) so this reuses it instead of duplicating the shape. **Needs attention** (`<section id="attention-section">`, first on Overview) is `needsAttention(input)` (`SERVERS_TAB`), pure and re-derived at every render (`renderNeedsAttention`, called from `loadStatus`/`reloadConfig`/`loadRouting`) — six kinds in a fixed order (`missing-setting`, `commands-refused`, `commands-unregistered`, `webhook-broken`, `home-missing`, `outdated-files`), each with a fix button that opens the right tab/card (`openPluginCard` reuses `refuseApply`'s own open-and-focus sequence via `cardHeaderEls`/`openCards`) or (for `outdated-files`) none at all — the banner already says it. **Pickers, not pasted ids**, for `DISCORD_SERVER_ID`/`ANNOUNCE_CHANNEL_ID`/`RELEASE_ANNOUNCE_CHANNEL_ID`: `FIELD_META` gains `control: "server"`/`control: "channel"`, `buildEnvControl`'s new branch builds a `<select>` (grouped `<optgroup>` by server for a channel picker) from the pure `pickerOptions(controlKind, discovery, value)` — taking the CONTROL KIND (`"server"`/`"channel"` from `FIELD_META[key].control`, `buildPickerSelect`'s job to look up), not the key name, so a future picker key needs no change there — through the SAME `id`/`dataset.key` common tail the plain-input branch uses — the Apply bar reads it exactly like the id field it replaces. Starts as the plain field whenever discovery is null or stale (`discoveryReadyForPickers`, #245's own `isDiscoveryStale` rule); `refreshEnvPickers()` (called from `loadRouting`) upgrades a plain field to its picker in place once discovery becomes ready, carrying the control's current value across. **Once shown, a picker never downgrades back to the plain field** (round-3/round-4/orchestrator-round findings, in that order): `buildEnvControl`'s own picker-vs-plain decision — separate from `refreshEnvPickers`'s own upgrade-only one, since `renderEnvFields()` rebuilds every field from scratch on any reload — checks a module-level `pickerKeysShown` Set rather than re-deciding fresh from current staleness each time, so it survives even a redraw that empties `#env-fields` some OTHER way than `renderEnvFields` itself (e.g. `reloadConfig`'s own catch, which writes plain error text over the container). **Nothing here touches the Apply bar's contract**: no `[data-key]`/`data-plugin`/`data-setting-key`/`data-secret-key` control anywhere in the Servers tab, and `onControlEdited` returns early for `.servers` right after its `.route` return (defensive — the `"#env-fields, #plugins-list"` check above it already excludes the tab in practice). Full shape: `docs/plans/epics/E236/10-servers-and-pickers.md`. See `ops/README.md`'s Admin panel section for the two-door auth model; **#404/#465/#466/#514:** `parsePluginIndex` refuses a non-array `env`/`commands`/`releases` (`null` too) and a duplicate name, so the tab shows the installed plugins with `indexError`; for an index that never went through the parse `mergePluginsView` reads the three containers through `asArray`, and `/api/plugins` drops a nameless status entry and, when the merge still throws, answers 200 with its own `indexError`; `entryFor` makes an unparsed duplicate last-wins (reachable from direct calls only: a parsed index has none); the asset fetch does not follow redirects and an unpinned `adminUrl` must sit under the plugin's own package; `serve()` wraps `handleRequest` with a 500 net and `SECURITY_HEADERS`, and `Bun.serve` takes `serveOptions` (`development: false`, `lastResortResponse` as the error handler); **#464:** the asset cap is enforced on the stream, asset fetches are cached (`withAssetCache`: a success 10 min, a failure 30 s, 32 entries, one in-flight fetch per URL) and a failed index load is remembered for 30 s, the drift banner is re-checked once a minute (`createOutdatedRefresher`, seeded from the boot list; a run that cannot tell keeps the last answer) and names install.sh's step 4 with `--force-recreate` for the compose half, the entry point's `HandlerConfig` literal is pinned by source |
| `ops/admin/admin-contract.ts` | The panel's own copy of the shape the plugins repo's `packages/api/admin.ts` defines — `ADMIN_API_VERSION` + the `AdminApi`/`SaveResult` types the server and `public/index.html`'s `makeAdminApi` need. Not `MountAdmin` (that type describes a plugin's own admin bundle, never imported by the panel) and, unlike `HOST_API_VERSION`'s cross-repo pin, deliberately **not** drift-tested against the plugins repo — a mismatch is safe-fail (the panel just refuses to mount), so this file's own header says to bump it in lockstep by hand instead. The admin-UI analog of `src/plugins/contract.ts`; kept in sync with the Dockerfile's `COPY` by the ratchet test (see the Gotchas entry), not by hand. See `docs/adr/0005` |
| `ops/admin/public/admin.css` | The panel's own layout and the few composites `@rackbops/styles` does not ship — the page column and header, the tab panels, the chip editor (`tag-field`), the plugin cards and update block (`.plug__*`, `.step`, `.secret` — #244), the `msg` notice and `adm-note` (**#300:** a plain secondary-text note describing no particular control — the panel's own class now that help/error text for a control comes from the theme's `rb-field__help`/`rb-field__error`, #210), and the bare `label`/`input`/`select`/`button` a plugin's own settings bundle builds inside its card's `.plug__admin` (selectors wholly inside `:where(.plug__admin …)`, so zero specificity and a bundle's own styling still wins). **#300:** `.plugin-incompat` keeps its own copy of the severity-bar declarations (never coloured small text) that used to be shared with the deleted `field-hint--danger`; `.adm [aria-invalid="true"]` is gone too — the theme's own `.rb-input[aria-invalid="true"]`/etc. own that now, and only the two composites the theme doesn't know about (`.tag-field:has([aria-invalid="true"])`, `.route__channels[aria-invalid="true"]`) keep a local rule. Written with `--rb-*` tokens only — no colour, `font-family` or `border-radius` literal, and no custom property of its own — pinned by `server.test.ts`'s "admin.css uses tokens only". It restates `[hidden] { display: none !important }`: an author `display` rule (`.adm`, `.rb-alert`) beats the browser's own `hidden` rule, so without it the hidden app (behind the token gate), the hidden tab panels and the drift banner would all show |
| `ops/admin/public/rb-theme.css` | **Generated — never hand-edit.** The `arcane-obsidian` (dark) and `arcane-parchment` (light) bundles of the pinned `@rackbops/styles`, concatenated by `ops/admin/theme/build-theme.ts` and committed; line 1 names the version it came from. The page picks between the two from `prefers-color-scheme` (an inline script sets `data-rb-style` on `<html>` before the stylesheets load; there is no toggle). See the Gotchas entry for the bump procedure |
| `ops/admin/theme/` | Build-only package (`package.json` pinning `@rackbops/styles` exactly, `build-theme.ts`, its test, a README) that writes `../public/rb-theme.css`. Nothing in it runs at deploy or runtime, and the admin image never copies it (the Dockerfile's `COPY` lines name only `package.json`/`bun.lock`, `server.ts`, `admin-contract.ts` and `public/`). `composeThemeCss()` is the pure half; the `import.meta.main` block reads the pin, refuses anything but an exact `x.y.z`, refuses an installed version that differs from the pin, and writes the file |
| `src/storage.ts` | Shared atomic-JSON-file primitives — `writeJsonAtomic`/`readJsonOrFresh` (and `writeFileAtomic`, the same write for text that is already serialized, #496 — the plugin index cache uses it) (the temp-file-then-rename + corrupt-file-moved-aside pattern #68 first put in `state.ts`, which now goes through these two) plus two ways to serialize concurrent access: `createJsonWriter` (one file, whole-value writes) and `createKeyedJsonMutator` (many files, each identified by path, and — unlike `createJsonWriter` — serializes the READ that precedes the write too, not just the write itself, so two concurrent read-modify-writes to the same file can't lose one). `writeJsonAtomic` alone is still NOT safe for two callers *in the same process* racing the same path — a lost-update risk from the two writes themselves interleaving, which only `createJsonWriter`/`createKeyedJsonMutator` actually serialize; a caller reaches it directly only through one of those two wrappers. Its temp NAME, though, is per-process-unique — `tmpPathFor`'s `${path}.${pid}.${TMP_TOKEN}.${counter}.tmp` (#154 replaced a fixed `${path}.tmp`; #253 added the per-process token) — see the gotcha. Also re-exports `resolveDataDir(env, entry)` from `src/dataDir.ts` (moved there in #493 so `bootLog.ts` can call it without evaluating this module's body) and exports the `DATA_DIR` it produces — `state.ts`/`handoff.ts`/`index.ts` all **import** it rather than recomputing it (#139; three separate spellings of the same path is what let the suite write the developer's real `data/`). Defaults to `join(import.meta.dir, "..", "data")`; a `BOT_DATA_DIR` override relocates the whole directory, absolute-only, and under `bun test` an unset override is a hard error rather than a silent fall back to the checkout. **A test seam, not an operator knob** — deliberately absent from `.env.example` and from `bot-ops.sh`'s whitelist; see the gotcha. Handed to plugins as `HostApi.dataDir`. **Moved up from `src/warbandeer/` in #99** so it is the host's storage module — its four primitives ARE `HostApi.storage`, now consumed by plugins (the warbandeer plugin's links/characters storage) rather than any in-tree code. Never recompute a data path from `import.meta.dir` under `src/plugins/` — that is two hops from `data/`, not one. **#509/#510/#511:** `readJsonOrFresh` moves aside only a file that parsed badly (`classifyReadError`: a `SyntaxError`), reads a vanished file as absent, and rethrows any other read error on a file that exists (EACCES, EIO) after one log line — for every caller, the installed plugins' `HostStorage.readJsonOrFresh` included, since returning fresh let the next write overwrite a file that was intact (`HOST_API_VERSION` and the contract are unchanged); `writeFileAtomic` removes its temp file when the write or rename fails; `moveCorruptAside` is the one move-aside (`state.ts` uses it for its shape check); `createKeyedJsonMutator` drops a path's queue once it settles (`pendingPaths()`); `TMP_TOKEN` is exported for its test. **#477:** `readJsonOrFresh` takes `ReadJsonOptions`: `onCorrupt` (`"move-aside"`, the default, for a serialized write that then replaces the file, or `"keep"`, for a plain read, which leaves an unparseable file in place and says so once per path per process) and `redactReason` (print only the error's name, never a message that can quote a secrets file); a kept path is remembered until a good parse, an absent file or a move-aside, so a second corruption episode is said again (`resetKeptCorruptForTest`, `storageStateForTest`, which the test-state guard reads). No `fsync` anywhere, the secrets file included (#510, #479) |

An absolute `BOT_DATA_DIR` wins and a relative one throws; with none set, `bun test` throws rather than fall back to the checkout's `data/` — recognised by `NODE_ENV=test` (which Bun sets only when the shell exported no `NODE_ENV`; `NODE_ENV=production bun test` keeps `production`) **or** by `process.argv[1]` being a `*.test.*`/`*.spec.*`/`*_test.*`/`*_spec.*` file (Bun's own discovery names); otherwise `<repo>/data`. | `src/plugins/index.ts` | `loadPluginIndex(url, dataDir, deps?)` — fetches the Plugin Index with a `timeoutMs`-bounded `AbortSignal` (default 5s; a bare absolute-path `url` is rewritten to `file://` first, since `fetch()` can't resolve a schemeless string), validates its shape — including that every entry's `intents` are numbers, that every `env` element is a `{ key }` object (#496: it is read for its key downstream), that every `releases` element is a `{ version, publishedAt }` object of strings with `url` and `notes` strings when present (#440: one bad release makes the whole index unusable, as a bad entry does), and that no two entries share a `name`, since discord.js throws on a non-number intent and a duplicate name would silently collapse in `registry.ts`'s `Map` lookup; one bad entry makes the whole index unusable, deliberately, and the log names the first failing check and entry (#496) — and on success atomically caches it to `data/plugins/index.json` wrapped as `{ writtenAt, index }` (`deps.now()` — deliberately distinct from the manifest's own `generatedAt`, which is the plugins repo CI's clock, not this bot's). A fetch-side failure falls back to the cached copy with a warning naming its cause (#496): `[plugins] index unreachable (…)` when no whole answer arrived (a rejected or timed-out fetch, a non-200, a body that stops arriving), `[plugins] index invalid (…)` when one did but is not JSON or not a usable index (the cached copy is held to the same check), or an empty index with a warning if there's no usable cache; a *cache-write* failure after an otherwise-good fetch is different — it keeps and returns the freshly-fetched data (`source: "fresh"`) with its own warning, since discarding good data over a volume write error would be worse than the write error itself. Either way — **never throws**, so a manifest outage degrades to "no new plugins," never a boot failure. `readFile`/`writeFile` default to real `Bun.file` and `src/storage.ts`'s `writeFileAtomic` (#496: it used to be a hand-kept copy that missed #253's per-process temp token; `src/plugins/*` still stays dependency-free of `config.ts`) but are injectable for tests |
| `src/plugins/index.test.ts` | bun tests (real temp dir via `mkdtempSync`, only `fetch`/`now` faked) for the full fallback matrix — fresh writes an atomic `{writtenAt, index}` cache; a rejected/timed-out fetch, a non-200, invalid JSON, and an invalid shape all fall back to the cache with the `writtenAt`-bearing warning; no cache or a corrupt cache falls back to empty; a cache-write failure after a good fetch still returns `"fresh"` rather than discarding it. **#496:** the warning says `invalid`, not `unreachable`, for a body that is not JSON (with the parse error) or fails the shape check (naming the check and the entry: a bad name, an unknown `schemaVersion`, an `env` element that is not a `{ key }` object, a duplicate name, a non-object entry); a body that stops arriving still says `unreachable`; a value is shown as JSON (`"1"` vs `1`, an object's contents) and bounded; a parseable cache that fails the shape check falls back to empty, naming why |
| `src/plugins/registry.ts` | Pure selection, no I/O. `selectPlugins(index, configured, hostApiVersion, coreCommandNames, installed?)` walks `configured` (parsed `PLUGINS=` tokens) in order, skipping — with an exact reason string, kept for later reporting by #99/#101/#102 — a name absent from the index, a `hostApiVersion` mismatch, an entry that declares a **privileged gateway intent** (`GuildMembers`, `GuildPresences`, `MessageContent`; a bit mask over each declared number, so `32770` is caught; the host never requests one, since one the operator has not enabled is refused at login and would crash-loop every instance with that plugin — #493; the reason names the intents and runs after the host-API check, so a host-API skip keeps its reason), or a command that collides with a core command or an earlier-selected plugin's. **An older pinned/last-good version can be kept past an index host-API bump (#222):** the index's `hostApiVersion` describes `entry.version` alone (`PluginRelease` carries none), so the host-API check is no longer an unconditional skip. When the index's current version needs a NEWER host than the bot's, the plugin is kept if every version `installPlugins` may try is provably older than `entry.version` — the explicit `PLUGINS=name@version` pin and only that, otherwise #104's `targetVersion` and, should that install fail, the last-good `installedVersion` (the optional `installed` map, built from the previous `state.json` by the pure, shape-tolerant `pinsFromState(state)`) — and the entry declares no intents (they would be unioned into the whole `Client` for a version that isn't the one running). Everything else stays skipped: no pin, a version equal to / newer than / unorderable against the current one, a current needing an OLDER host than the bot's, an entry with intents. Not modelled: `installPlugins`' newest-cached fallback (it needs disk access). **#223: `install.ts`'s `reconcileManifest` checks a kept version's own declared `hostApiVersion` against this host's `HOST_API_VERSION` (not `entry.hostApiVersion`, which describes only the index's current version) on both the cache-reuse and post-extract paths, and refuses a mismatch** — a `commands`/`env` divergence only warns, and a missing/unparseable `package.json` never refuses. A skipped plugin still comes back in the result; `entry` is only absent for the "not in the plugin index" case, since every other skip reason still has a real `PluginIndexEntry` to attach. `collectIntents(core, selected)` unions `core` with every non-skipped plugin's declared intents, deduped, first-seen order — this is what lets `src/index.ts` size the discord.js `Client`'s intents to exactly what the selected plugins need, before any plugin code runs. `describeSkips(selected)` renders each skip as a `"<name>: <reason>"` line, kept as a pure function (rather than logging inline here) so the exact reason text stays unit-testable without a console spy — `src/index.ts` is what actually logs them |

| `src/plugins/install.ts` | Installs the plugins `selectPlugins` chose (#99): resolves `package@version` on the registry (`https://registry.npmjs.org`, or `PLUGIN_REGISTRY_URL` in dev — a blank or whitespace-only value is treated as unset — broader than `PLUGIN_INDEX_URL`, where only a blank one is (#281, #436)), downloads the tarball, verifies `dist.integrity` (SHA-512 SRI, from the same registry response as the tarball URL — it catches a corrupt download, not a hostile registry, #497) BEFORE extract, and extracts the bundle into a sibling staging dir (`<name>/.staging-<version>-<now>/`), reconciles it (below), then renames it into place as `data/plugins/<name>/<version>/dist/plugin.js` (+ `package.json`; `tar --strip-components=1` drops only the tarball's leading `package/`, so `plugin.js` keeps its `dist/` level) — **#224: extract-then-rename, never extract-straight-into-`versionDir`**, so anything at `<version>/dist/plugin.js` came from a completed extract and a `tar` that dies mid-write leaves nothing reusable; any earlier partial `versionDir` is cleared first, and the staging dir is always removed in a `finally` (a no-op once the rename has moved it). Version resolution: operator pin (`PLUGINS=name@version`) → **#104's transient `targetVersion`** (an explicit `/plugins update`) → previous `state.json` `installedVersion` → newest cached (a dir named like a version, ordered by `updates.ts`'s `compareSemver`, #497) → index's current — the bot NEVER moves an installed plugin to a newer version on its own. **#496:** whichever version that yields must match `PLUGIN_VERSION_RE` (the shape `config.ts` requires of a pin) before it reaches a path, the registry URL or an `rmSync`; anything else (an index `latest`, a corrupt `state.json`'s `..`) is skipped with a reason. **#497:** the tarball download is capped at `MAX_TARBALL_BYTES` (20 MiB) and the registry metadata at `MAX_METADATA_BYTES` (1 MiB), both through `readCapped` (a declared `Content-Length` over the cap is refused unread, any other body abandoned once it passes it); `tarExtract` runs `tar` through `runBounded`, which gives up after `TAR_TIMEOUT_MS` (60 s), SIGKILLs it, cancels its stderr read and waits on neither its exit nor its stderr closing (a child `tar` forked can outlive it holding that pipe). **#498:** `installPlugins` first sweeps an interrupted install's leftovers — a `.staging-*` dir under any `plugins/<name>/` and a tarball in `plugins/tmp/` (both kinds are matched there, so a plugin named `tmp` is swept like any other); a dir that cannot be listed or a leftover that cannot be removed is warned about and left, a missing `plugins/` (a fresh install) is silent. A cached bundle is reused with no fetch. **#223: `reconcileManifest`** (exported, unit-tested directly) reconciles the extracted bundle's own `package.json` `botPlugin` block against the host on BOTH the cache-reuse fast path (before reuse) and a fresh extract (against the staged copy, before the rename commits it) — total, never throws: a missing/unreadable/unparseable `package.json` or one with no `botPlugin` block warns and installs; `commands`/`env` key-set divergence from the index entry warns; a `hostApiVersion` that differs from this host's `HOST_API_VERSION` (deliberately NOT `entry.hostApiVersion`, which describes only the index's current version — #222's `keepOlder` can legitimately install an older version built against a different host API) refuses the bundle, recorded in `skips`/`fallbacks` like any other install failure. **#104 failure fallback:** when a `targetVersion` install fails (including a `reconcileManifest` refusal), it reverts to the recorded `installedVersion` (deterministic — NOT `newestCachedVersion`, which could out-rank a hand-lowered pin), so the bot comes back on the previous version (installed again, normally from its cached bundle; it can still fail to load or activate, in which case its entry is `active: false` and the report-back says so, #543); recorded in `InstallResult.fallbacks`. Any per-plugin failure with no usable fallback is recorded in `skips` and affects only that plugin. `newestCachedVersion` counts only a dir whose name matches `PLUGIN_VERSION_RE`, so neither a crashed extract's `.staging-*` leftover nor `tmp` is ever taken for a cached version. Pure over injected `{ fetch, extract, now, log }`; `tarExtract` (production `extract`) shells `tar` (present in `oven/bun:1-slim`). No plugin code runs here |
| `src/plugins/install.test.ts` | bun tests: registry URL + encoded package/version, SRI verified (mismatch → skipped, nothing extracted), cache reused with no fetch, pin/previous-installed version wins over the index's newer one, 404 skipped with a reason while others install, selection-skipped plugins ignored; **#104: a broken `targetVersion` reverts to the recorded previous (never a newer cached one — the silent-upgrade guard), and no cached previous is a plain skip**. **#224: a part-way extract leaves nothing reusable and re-fetches next time, a leftover `.staging-*` dir is never treated as a cached version, the bundle is extracted to staging and moved into place (not written to `versionDir` directly), and an earlier partial `versionDir` is replaced, not merged.** **#223: a `hostApiVersion` mismatch is refused (and never lands) on both the fresh-extract and cached-reuse paths, with a reason naming both numbers; | `src/plugins/host.ts` | Loads/wires/activates the installed bundles, inside `activate()` after `takeOver()`. `createHostApi` builds a plugin's `HostApi` — `env` is ONLY the keys the entry declares (never the whole environment), `log` prefixes `[name] ` (and, since #545, `log.error` hands the base logger `withoutRequest(err)`, so a failed call's error a plugin logs loses its `url`, `path` and `requestBody`; the plugin's own error object is untouched). `loadPlugins` imports each bundle and runs `createPlugin(host)`, isolating a throwing import/createPlugin, and an import still unsettled after `PLUGIN_IMPORT_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s, #408), the same way (recorded, skipped, never fatal). `pluginCommandMap` maps bare→command, dropping a whole plugin's set on any collision with core or an earlier plugin (defence in depth behind `selectPlugins`). `buildCommandBody(prefix, coreJson, map)` = core JSON then each plugin command built via `commandNamer(prefix)` (dropping a builder that produced the wrong name). `pluginTicks` wraps each plugin tick so it runs only while that plugin's `running` flag is set, and bounds the wait on each at `PLUGIN_TICK_TIMEOUT_MS` (30 s, #217): a tick that hasn't settled by then rejects, `runTick` logs it as `[tick:<plugin>:<tick>]` and the plugins behind it still run. At the bound the call's `AbortSignal` is aborted (#248); a call that ignores it keeps running — concurrently with whatever runs next, that plugin's other ticks included — so `pluginTicks` skips that tick, with a `[plugins] <plugin>:<tick>: its previous tick is still running` warning, until the call settles, instead of re-entering it: a merely slow tick recovers, one that never settles silences that tick (its siblings keep running) until the bot restarts. **#248:** every call also holds a unit of `restart.ts`'s critical section until it settles, through the `PluginTickControl` `index.ts` wires to `onStopRequested`: a restart request or a `SIGTERM` aborts every pending call and stops new ones starting, and releases each hold when its call settles or after `PLUGIN_TICK_ABORT_GRACE_MS` (5 s), whichever is first (see `restart.ts`'s header). `activatePlugins` runs each `activate()` in order, isolating a throw, and an `activate()` still unsettled after `PLUGIN_ACTIVATE_TIMEOUT_MS` (30 s, #408) the same way: that plugin stays off this boot (its slash commands included: they are `running`-gated too since #407), and if its `activate()` resolves later, its `dispose()` runs then (`afterLateActivate`), since `disposePlugins` skips a plugin that was never marked running. **#541:** `disposePlugins` first sets a module-level `disposing` flag, synchronously, which `activatePlugins` reads before each plugin's `activate()` (a shutdown began: the plugin is not activated and its `error` says so) and after it settles inside the bound (it came up while the process was leaving: its `dispose()` runs, awaited and bounded by `PLUGIN_DISPOSE_TIMEOUT_MS` (an optional fourth parameter, the test seam), isolated like every other plugin call, and the plugin stays off); `activatePlugins` publishes its run (`activation`, settled and never rejecting) so `disposePlugins` also waits for the activation in flight, concurrently with the other disposals and under its own `timeoutMs` (a wedged `activate()` is given up on like a wedged `dispose()`; the late dispose has its own bound, the same 2 s in production), because `activate()` holds no critical section and `exit(0)` follows within seconds. Both are guarded state; `resetPluginDisposingForTest()` clears only the flag (a test's `afterEach` after `disposePlugins`), `resetPluginHostForTest()` also forgets the run. Every catch here that builds its own message text from what it caught (a log line, or `state.json`'s `error`) does it through `describeThrown`, which never throws itself, so even a value `String()` can't convert, or an Error whose `message` getter throws, stays isolated; the catches that hand the raw value to `log.error(message, err)` instead rely on the logger to print it. **#220 (ADR-0007):** `routeHttpRequest(loaded, request, clientIp, log, timeoutMs?, maxBodyBytes?)` answers one request on the host's HTTP listener. The first path segment names the plugin, matched exactly, and the rest becomes `info.path` for its `http(request, info)`. It answers `404` for no segment or an unknown plugin, and `503` for a plugin that is not running, checked before any of its code (getter included) runs. It answers `404` for a running plugin with no `http` handler, and `413` for a body over `HTTP_MAX_BODY_BYTES` (1 MiB), declared or chunked: the router reads it with a running count and hands the plugin a new `Request` over the buffered bytes, because Bun's `maxRequestBodySize` bounds only a body with a `Content-Length`. It answers `500` for a throw (a throwing getter included) or a non-`Response` result, with no plugin text in the body; `504` once the handler has run for `PLUGIN_HTTP_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s), without cancelling the call; and `400` for an unparseable URL or a body stream that fails mid-read. The plugin always gets a rebuilt `Request` (#471): over the buffered body, with the client's abort signal, with `CF-Connecting-IP` and `X-Forwarded-For` set to the vetted `clientIp` and `Forwarded`/`X-Real-IP`/`True-Client-IP`/`CF-Connecting-IPv6` removed (#472), and with `Content-Length` set to the buffered length (no `Transfer-Encoding`), or neither without a body. **#470/#471 hardening:** `pluginCommandMap` also drops a plugin's whole set when it returns two commands of one name, reserves every loaded plugin's declared names first, so a command a plugin returns but does not declare is dropped on its own when another plugin declares it (whichever loads first) and otherwise registered with a warning, and names who holds a collided name; `post` returns the guild the message actually landed in (`sent.guildId`), warning when routing put it in another server, or in none (the caller's guildId is kept then); `dispatchPluginInteractionOutcome` reads the `interactions` getter inside its try, and that catch and `dispatchPluginAutocomplete`'s log the error through `withoutRequest` (#545); and each `pluginTicks` wait registers its own grace waiter through `PluginTickControl.onGraceElapsed` and removes it when the wait ends, instead of chaining on one shared pending promise per run. **#184:** `disposePlugins(loaded, log, timeoutMs)` is `activatePlugins`'s counterpart, called once on the way out (`shutdown.ts`'s drain) — disposes only plugins that are actually `running`, each under its own `PLUGIN_DISPOSE_TIMEOUT_MS`-bounded window (one wedged plugin can't block the rest), isolating a throw — sync, async, or a malformed non-function `dispose` — exactly like `activatePlugins` isolates a throwing `activate()`; flips `running=false` for every plugin it touches BEFORE any `dispose()` is called (#409), so from the moment disposal begins every running gate (ticks, HTTP, slash commands, components, modals, autocomplete) refuses the plugin rather than reach a resource dispose is releasing, has released, or failed to release. **#407:** `findPluginCommand(map, loaded, bare)` is the lookup `index.ts` hands `handleCommand`: the plugin command `bare` names, the plugin that owns it, and a `running()` that reads that plugin's flag at call time. `readPluginState`/`writePluginState`/`buildPluginStateFile` produce `data/plugins/state.json`, preserving the operator-bookkeeping fields (`notifiedVersion`/`skippedVersion`/`remindAt`/`scheduled`/`pendingReport`) from the previous file and **consuming #104's transient `targetVersion`** (success → it becomes `installedVersion` + is dropped; failure → `installedVersion` stays the previous, `error` names the failed target, `targetVersion` still dropped so a broken target never retries forever — threaded via `InstallResult.fallbacks`). `mutatePluginState` is the ONE race-safe runtime read-modify-write of `state.json` (a shared `createKeyedJsonMutator`), used by #103's update tick **and #104's `/plugins` actions + boot report-back** — the boot `writePluginState` is a one-time whole-file write ordered before any core tick that touches `state.json` (#103's update pass waits on the "state ready" flag `index.ts` sets after it; plugin ticks can already have run, but never write it, #471). All pure/DI'd |

Both do nothing before `initRouting` and never reject. Everything that registers or rewrites discovery runs on ONE promise chain, so a second call queues behind the first — the request mailbox (#241) calls `applyRouting` (after a `routing-set`) and `refreshDiscovery` (for a `discovery-refresh`, and once before refusing a request for something the file does not show) while a boot registration or a refresh may be in flight. In `single` mode a failure is rethrown once `discovery.json` is written (it is not, when the servers could not be read), so `index.ts`'s own `catch` and its operator message still run; it is written against the home server's entry when the bot can see that server, while a failure of the global scope (no home server) has no entry to go on and reaches the operator through that message only. `routed` mode throws only if reading routing does (a read error that is not a parse error is rethrown by `readJsonOrFresh`, #509). A discovery write failure is logged and swallowed, and a read of the bot's servers that failed writes nothing (the panel keeps the last copy; a routed run registers nowhere). In `routed` mode it also warns, once per distinct message per process (`resetRoutingForTest` clears it; `routingStateForTest` is what the test-state guard reads), when `DISCORD_SERVER_ID` names a server the bot is not in and some loaded plugin that has commands is unplaced, since those commands are then registered nowhere (#260); nothing is said when the server list could not be read, when there is no home server, or when every plugin with commands is placed. **#420/#421/#482/#484/#485:** `applyRouting` reads the previous `discovery.json` (a failed read is said and treated as none), registers, says a failed empty, and keeps `lastRegistrations` when a routed run could not read the servers; `applyRoutingFor(guildId)` registers a joined server alone and merges its outcome (falling back to the full run until one has completed in this process, and when nothing is placed, the servers are unreadable or the guild is not among them); `logRouted` and both catches go through `tell`, so a throwing logger no longer rejects a routed registration; `logName` makes a server name one line of at most 80 characters. Tests: `live.test.ts`. |
| `src/routing/requests.ts` | **#241.** The four routing actions of the request mailbox (`ROUTING_ACTIONS`): `routing-set` (one plugin's server map), `webhook-add`, `webhook-remove`, `discovery-refresh`. `parseRoutingRequest` is pure, shape only, and cannot throw on any JSON value (it never walks into one); its reasons name the field and never quote the value, and a malformed `id` is dropped, not fatal. `applyRoutingRequest(request, deps)` validates against `discovery.json` -- after ONE `refreshDiscovery()` and a re-check, so a server joined a minute ago is not refused for missing from an old file -- writes the validator's clean value (never the raw input) and stamps `updatedBy`; a `routing-set` then awaits `applyRouting` (its rejection is logged and swallowed), so its result is recorded only once the re-registration has run: a slow one can outlast the panel's 30 s window, which reports a timeout for a change that did land (recording earlier would show the panel a wrong view, #484). A `discovery-refresh` is refused when `refreshDiscovery` wrote nothing. The `webhook-add` reason for a channel not in discovery says the bot lists only the text and announcement channels it can see. `webhook-add` matches `WEBHOOK_URL_RE`, asks Discord (`liveFetchWebhook`: `GET https://discord.com/api/v10/webhooks/<id>/<token>` built from the two captured groups, never the pasted string; 10 s; a redirect is an error; the fetch error is dropped and the body never returned beyond three ids), requires that it posts into a server and channel in discovery, then writes `routing.secrets.json` FIRST (the canonical URL) and `routing.json` second; `webhook-remove` is the reverse and clears whichever of routing.json and the secrets file (`readSecrets`) holds the channel, so a half-removed webhook can be removed again; a failed second write of an add puts the previous secret back. One webhook per channel: an add replaces the old one and clears `broken`. `RoutingRefusal` is an expected "no" whose reason is shown as it is. `redactWebhookUrls` is the belt behind the braces: every reason passes through it, and so does `requestedBy`, which lands in `routing.json` (a webhook-add's own token is also taken out of `requestedBy` and `id` wherever it sits). Tests: `requests.test.ts`, and `secrets-leak.test.ts`, the acceptance test that searches every file under a real data dir and every captured log line for every scenario's token. |
| `src/plugins/drain.ts` | **#241.** `startRequestDrain(opts)` -- the request mailbox drained every `REQUEST_DRAIN_MS` (5 s) on its own `setInterval`, so a panel change shows up while the operator is still looking; the 60-second `pluginRequests` tick check stays as the backstop. A beat does nothing when `!ready()` (the boot state write and boot drain have not landed), when `restartPending()`, or while the drain it started last is still running (a slow Discord lookup cannot queue a backlog); a drain that throws is logged and the next beat still drains. **#508:** a beat waits on one drain, and holds `opts.hold` for it, for at most `REQUEST_DRAIN_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 min); past that it logs, lets go and the next beat tries again (the drain is not cancelled, and `consumePluginRequests` queues behind it, so one that never settles keeps the mailbox stalled, but logged every bound). The 60-second tick's `pluginRequests` pass, which queues behind the same drain inside the tick's own critical section, waits through `settledWithin` too (exported for it), for at most `announce.ts`'s `PLUGIN_REQUESTS_TICK_WAIT_MS` (2 min, below the tick watchdog's 5), so a beat holds a restart for at most its bound and a tick for its own plus the rest of the tick; a slow drain cut loose by either can be interrupted by a restart, and the boot drain replays its request (writes are temp-then-rename, the request file removed only once applied). Deliberately outside the tick machinery. Started from `index.ts` right after `startScheduler(...)`, gated on plugin state (`isPluginStateReady`) and `restartPending`, each drain held under `withCritical` through `hold` (so a restart an update-now asks for waits for it, for at most the bound). Tests: `drain.test.ts` (through the `schedule` seam; the default is pinned through spies on `setInterval` / `clearInterval`, so no test waits on a wall clock beyond the #508 bound tests' tens of milliseconds, which are deterministic; #508: a drain that never settles is let go at the bound, logged, the hold released and the next beat drains; one that settles or rejects inside it releases the hold then; one that rejects after it is dropped; no bound timer is left behind; the default bound; `settledWithin` itself). |

## Behavior

- **Dedup keys** in `BotState`: `seenReleaseIds` (`Record<owner/repo, number[]>`, each capped at 100).
  A restart never re-announces a release whose save landed before the new process started: each id is persisted right after its post (`commitReleaseAnnouncements`), and a walk stops between repos once a restart or shutdown is pending (`checkReleases`, #499; the repos it did not reach are polled by the next walk: the next boot's startup catch-up, or the next 15-minute poll when the process carries on after a failed handoff). What can still re-post a release: an exit in the instant between its post and its save; a save that fails or never reaches disk and is followed by a restart before a later save succeeds (the id is already in memory, so the same process does not post it twice); a send that errors client-side after Discord accepted it; or two walks overlapping (`github.ts`'s header). A replacement booting from a stale `state.json` snapshot used to be one more (#507); since #507 `takeOver` re-reads the file once the original is stopped. The WoW dedup keys (`dmfAnnouncedFor`, `weeklyAnnouncedFor`, `realmStatus`)
  were removed from `BotState` in #107 — the wow plugin owns them in its own `data/wow.json` now; any
  copies a prior version left in `state.json` are preserved untouched (the `{ ...raw }` load spread +
  `saveState`'s whole-object write), never read by the core again.
- **Realm watch** is the `wow` plugin's now (#107), not the core's: whenever Blizzard creds +
  `WOW_REALM` are configured it polls every 2 min and announces every UP↔DOWN transition. Its
  last-reading dedup lives in the plugin's own `data/wow.json` (seeded once from the legacy
  `state.json` copy), so the first observation seeds silently; a restart reuses the stored status, so it never *repeats*
  an already-announced transition but does announce a real one that occurred while the bot was offline.
  `ops/bot-ops.sh status` reads `wow.json` first, falling back to `state.json`, for the `realmStatus`
  field the panel + `wow-companion` consume.
- **Release polling** runs on a flat 15-min cadence plus a startup catch-up (there is no release
  cron in this fork — same shape as the self-update / plugin-update checks). Each repo in `config.watchedRepos` is polled
  independently; a repo's first-ever poll (its key absent from `seenReleaseIds`) seeds silently.
- **Command registration** (#239) decides at boot whether routing exists. `applyRouting` reads
  `data/routing.json` (with `readRouting`: a missing or wrong-shaped file is fresh, an unparseable
  one is left in place and read as fresh, said once, and the next write moves it aside (#477); any other read error rejects, #509) and hands it to the pure `planRegistration`. If it places no plugin — no file, an empty one, or
  one holding only webhook metadata — the plan is `single`: the same one PUT the bot always made (to
  `DISCORD_SERVER_ID`'s guild, or globally when that is unset, preceded once by the empties a routed past
  requires, #420) and the same `Registered N slash commands` line. If it places at least one plugin the plan is `routed`: one PUT per server the bot is
  in, one server at a time, in server-name order — the core commands plus the commands of every plugin
  that lives there — then one `Registered commands in N servers (...)` line, plus a `[routing]
  couldn't register commands in <name> (<id>): <error>` line per refusal. A refusal in one server
  (Discord's 50001, no `applications.commands` scope) is recorded against it in `discovery.json` and
  stops neither the other servers nor the boot; in `single` mode the failure still reaches
  `index.ts`'s long `catch`. With no `DISCORD_SERVER_ID` in routed mode the global command list is
  emptied after the servers, or every command would show twice — but only when at least one server
  took its commands: if none did, or the servers could not be read, it is left as it was (it is all any
  server has) and, when servers refused, the log says so. "At least one" is deliberate: if every server
  had to succeed, one that refuses for good (50001) would keep the global list, and every command
  would show twice, on every boot. The price is that a server that failed for a passing reason while
  others succeeded has no commands until the next full registration run (the next boot, or a routing change
  from the panel; a join registers only the joined server, so it no longer retries the others); it is in the
  log and in `discovery.json` with its error. `data/discovery.json` is written after every registration whose server list could be
  read — the boot one included, routing or not — and refreshed every 15 minutes by the `discovery`
  tick; a failure to write it is logged, never thrown. A server the bot **joins** after boot (#259,
  discord.js `guildCreate`) runs `applyRoutingFor` in routed mode — that server's registration alone, which also
  rewrites `discovery.json` (the file the panel reads), so it lists the server within seconds — and, in single mode, only a discovery refresh: single mode registers to one place (the home
  server, or globally when there is none), which a join does not change, except that a join **of the home
  server** runs the registration again (a bot that was not in it at boot, so that registration was refused,
  and is invited later, or that was removed from it and is added back, has nothing there until it does;
  Discord is understood to drop a server's commands when the bot leaves it, which is not verified against
  a live server. Re-authorizing a bot that is still a member, the usual cure for a 50001, emits no join at
  all and waits for the next registration). With a home server set, any other joined server gets no
  commands in single mode, as before; with none, the global list already reaches it. A server it
  **leaves** (`guildDelete`) refreshes discovery and nothing else: `routing.json` is not touched, so being
  kicked and re-invited loses no placement (a server that is in routing but not in discovery is one the
  panel has to be able to show as unavailable; that view is #246's, and its issue text does not yet list
  this case). Neither does anything before `initRouting`.
- **The request mailbox** (#105, #241) is `data/plugins/requests/*.json`, drained in filename order by
  one single-flight consumer: at boot (after `initRouting`, before `markPluginStateReady()`), on every
  60-second tick, and every five seconds on `startRequestDrain`'s timer once the boot has landed. Nine
  actions: the five update ones on an installed plugin, and `routing-set`, `webhook-add`,
  `webhook-remove` and `discovery-refresh` against servers and channels the bot can see; nothing here
  enables a plugin. A routing request may carry an `id` (8-64 of `A-Za-z0-9_-`) that the panel chose,
  and once it is applied or refused its outcome is appended to `routing.json`'s `results` under that id
  (a request with no valid id records nothing). The Plugin Index is fetched only when a drain holds a
  file that parses, is not a routing request, and holds nothing that looks like a webhook URL (one that
  does is refused before the index is needed); if it cannot be loaded those files stay queued and the
  drain fails, as it always has, but the routing requests behind them are still handled and a file that
  will not parse is still rejected. A file that will not parse is looked at a second time (once, after
  `tornReadRetryMs`, 250 ms) before it is rejected, because a writer older than #240 writes with a
  non-atomic `cat > file` (an instance can run this bot with that older `ops/bot-ops.sh` until
  `install.sh` is re-run; it can only write the five update actions, so the half-written file is never a
  `webhook-add`) and the timer makes reading a half-written request likely enough to matter. That wait
  is paid once per drain, by the first such file: a later unparseable file in the same drain is looked at
  again at once
  (waiting for each would stall a mailbox of broken files), so the retry covers a write that finishes
  within the wait, for the first such file, and is best-effort for any later one. A request that
  was applied (a routing request or an update request), or any file that was refused, and cannot be
  deleted is remembered, with the text read (or as unread), and is not handled again: each drain only
  retries the delete, and a different text under the same name is handled as the new request it is
  (before #263 the update actions were not covered: an applied update request whose file could not be
  deleted was applied again on every drain, and an `update-now` asked for a restart each time). The
  memory is in-process only: `PluginStateFile` lives in `contract.ts`, which another repo vendors
  verbatim, and has nowhere to keep a list. So across a restart the loop is ended a different way:
  `validate` refuses an `update-now` or `schedule` for the version that is already installed
  (`<plugin> is already on <version>`, placed after the version, schedule-time and days checks so a
  malformed version is still "bad version"), and `skip`, `remind` and `cancel` for the installed version
  still apply. What that leaves: an undeletable file AND an installed version that never becomes the
  requested one (the install fails and falls back, is skipped at selection, or an operator's
  `PLUGINS=name@version` pin wins over the request) is one restart per boot, as it was. Likewise
  unchanged: an undeletable `update-now` still in the mailbox after a later upgrade past its version is
  replayed once against the newer install (it pins the older version and restarts, and is refused once
  that lands), and a stale `schedule` is applied again after a restart.
- **Self-update** asks whether the baked-in `GIT_SHA` **contains** the newest `BOT_BRANCH` (default
  `main`) commit on `GITHUB_REPO` (flat 15-min cadence + startup, only when `AUTO_UPDATE=true`;
  `/update` checks on demand with `force`). Stale → persist `attemptedUpdateToSha` (and the
  `/update` follow-up, and `swapInFlight` on the socket path), then `applyUpdate()`: a
  self-contained `redeploy()` when the daemon socket is mounted, else exit 75 for the orchestrator
  to respawn. `/update` is gated on the `ADMIN_USER_IDS` allowlist and fails
  closed when empty.
- **Update follow-up** closes the loop `/update` used to leave open: the command also persists a
  `pendingUpdateReport` (requester + both shas), and the next boot messages that requester with
  the build it actually came back on — `updated` / `noop` / `interrupted` / `rolled-back` / `unexpected` named
  explicitly (#450: a boot that finds a socket-path swap it started and never finished, with no live
  `<name>-next`, says `interrupted` (saved on the report, so it survives a restart before delivery) and clears the attempt marker instead of calling it a no-op; one
  that finds the `-next` alive, or cannot tell, leaves the report for the new build or the next
  boot), since the no-op is otherwise indistinguishable from success. Only a `/update`-initiated restart leaves
  a report, so `AUTO_UPDATE` exits and host reboots stay silent.
- **Character linking** is now the `@rackbops/plugin-warbandeer` plugin (issue #3, extracted in #100),
  installed with `PLUGINS=warbandeer` and off unless `WARBANDEER_INGEST_PORT` is set. When on: `/link`
  → the desktop app redeems the code for a Device Token → every later character push replaces that
  Account Label's prior Character Snapshot wholesale (never merged). The `/link`/`/unlink` commands,
  reply strings, wire contract, and `data/links.json` + `data/characters/` are identical to the old
  baked-in connector. See `docs/adr/0001`–`0003` for why it's shaped this way.
- **Plugins** (`PLUGINS=`, #99): at boot, before the `Client`, the Plugin Index is fetched and
  `selectPlugins` picks/validates the named plugins (intents unioned onto the Client; a plugin whose index entry needs a different host API is skipped — unless it is pinned, or last-good in the previous `state.json`, on a provably older version and the entry needs a NEWER host and declares no intents, in which case that older version is kept, #222). Then inside
  `activate()` (after `takeOver()`) each selected plugin is installed from npm (checked against the registry's own `dist.integrity` — the download matches what the registry served, not an independent hash, #497 —
  cached under `data/plugins/<name>/<version>/`, the installed version pinned — never auto-upgraded),
  `import()`ed, `createPlugin(host)`'d, its commands merged into the registration body, its ticks
  gathered for the scheduler, and `activate()` run — each step isolated so a bad plugin is skipped,
  never fatal. `data/plugins/state.json` records the outcome per plugin. An unset `PLUGINS` is a
  complete no-op: `Registered 3 slash commands` (the core set is `/report`, `/update`, `/plugins`, after
  #100 moved the connector's `/link`+`/unlink` into the warbandeer plugin and #107 moved
  `/dmf`/`/reset`/`/status`/`/transmog` into the wow plugin) — `PLUGINS=warbandeer` adds
  `/link`+`/unlink` back from the published plugin.
- **Plugin updates** (#103): *the operator's copy changes only when the operator says so.* A 15-min
  scheduler tick (`pluginUpdates`, with a startup catch-up like releases) re-fetches the Plugin Index
  and, for each installed plugin with a strictly-newer version, DMs the admins **once** — with the
  release notes between installed and available, and the choices `/plugins update|remind|skip` (or the
  panel). The **notification** never installs, restarts, or moves a pin — it only records
  `notifiedVersion` in `state.json` after a successful DM (channel fallback once, then capped retries).
  An incompatible newer version is surfaced worded "needs a newer bot", never offered for install. The
  tick is gated (`markPluginStateReady`) until the boot `state.json` write lands, so its keyed-mutator
  persist can't race that one-time whole-file write. `/plugins list` (admin-only, ephemeral) shows
  installed vs available + the first release note per plugin (and a pending schedule, if any), and marks
  an enabled plugin that is not running this boot (`active: false`) with `— not running (first error: <its error>)`
  on its first line, the error collapsed to one line and cut to 120 characters (#544). That `error` is
  the first failure recorded this boot, so on a reverted update it can be the target's install failure
  rather than why the previous version is down. The list shows whatever `state.json` holds when the
